5.3
Real Data Example: Application to Calcium Imaging Data

The two-photon calcium imaging technology can record in vivo functional activities of thousands of neurons
[
43
]
, and such data sets can be used to understand the neuronal circuits with the help of graphical model techniques
[
51
,
47
]
. In this section, we investigate the potential of our GI-JOE approach on a real calcium imaging data set from the Allen Institute
[
32
]
, which contains the functional recordings of
p=227
neurons in a mouse’s visual cortex, when different visual stimuli or no stimulus were presented to the mouse. Here, we focus on the raw fluorescence traces in one spontaneous session where there is no external stimulus. This session includes
n=8931
samples of the trace data associated with all
227
neurons. We manually mask the data for some neurons to create erose measurements, and then validate our methods by comparing the tested graph based on masked data with the tested graph based on the full data set. In particular, the recorded neurons all lie on the same vertical plane in a mouse’s visual cortex (see Figure
4
for the physical locations of the neurons in
x
and
y
axis). To manually create erose measurements, we divide the neurons into three subsets based on their location on the
x
-axis (marked in different colors in Figure
4
), and neurons in each subset are randomly observed with high (
\sqrt{0.9}
), moderate (
\sqrt{0.5}
), and low (
\sqrt{0.1}
) probabilities.

However, when inference methods are applied on the full data set, we find that the tested graph is always dense. This might be due to the huge amount of latent neurons in the mouse’s brain since latent variables are known to lead to dense graph structures in graphical models
[
51
]
. Since most of these edges have small edge weights, here we consider testing if the edge weights are stronger than a threshold instead of testing if they are zero. Specifically, for any node pair
(a,b)
,
H_{0,(a,b)}:|\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}|\leq 0.12
. Our GI-JOE approach can be directly extended to test such hypothesis and the detailed procedures are included in Appendix
E
.
For validation purposes, we also apply a special version of our GI-JOE (FDR) approach on the full data set, where all pairwise sample sizes are equal. As suggested by Figure
4
, our GI-JOE (FDR) approach works well, especially for the neuron set 1 (red) as they have larger sample sizes; it identifies the same hub neuron in set 1 as the tested graph (a) with the full data set. The specific F1-scores of each method for each neuron set can be found in Appendix
G
.

(a)

FDR-selected graph with full data

(b)

GI-JOE (FDR), applied to data with erose measurements

(c)

Debiased graphical lasso with minimum sample size, applied to data with erose measurements

Figure 4:
Tested functional connectivity graphs among neurons, using the Allen Brain Atlas data set. The neurons marked in red, blue, and green have high, moderate and low sample sizes. Our GI-JOE (FDR) approach works reasonably well, especially for red neurons, while the debiased graphical lasso with minimum sample size is too conservative to find any edge.

6
Discussion

In this paper, we propose the
GI-JOE
(
G
raph
I
nference when
J
oint
O
bservations are
E
rose) approach to address the graphical model inference problem under the
erose
measurement setting, where irregular observational patterns lead to vastly different sample sizes for node pairs. Our GI-JOE approach quantifies the different uncertainty levels over the graph induced by the erose measurements, including both an edge-wise inference method and an FDR control procedure. We characterize the Type I error and power for testing any node pair
(a,b)
by considering the sample sizes involving
a,b
’s neighbors; We also guarantee the valid FDR control of GI-JOE (FDR) under appropriate conditions. Finally, our experiments with synthetic and real data demonstrate the efficacy of our approach for different graphs and measurement patterns.

Graph learning with erose measurements is a common but understudied problem, and there are still many open questions for future investigation. For instance, it is possible to extend our theory and methods to general sub-Gaussian data or semiparametric graphical models. Our problem setting is closely related to the latent variable graphical models when some pairwise sample sizes are extremely low, and some ideas in this line of work might be useful to further improve our method with relaxed sample size conditions. In addition, our current results are based on a variant of the neighborhood lasso, while one may also consider a variant of the graphical lasso or CLIME and investigate their potential in the erose measurement setting. Another practical but challenging setting not considered in this paper is data-dependent erose measurements, which requires novel methods and theory since the sample covariance matrix would be biased in this setting and the plug-in type approach no longer works. Furthermore, erosely measured data sometimes take the form of a time series, and the temporal dependence could have an effect on the edge-wise variance computation, calling for new inference methodologies.

Appendix A
Optimization Algorithm for the Weighted
\ell_{\infty}
Norm Projection

We provide an ADMM algorithm to solve the following problem:

\widetilde{\Sigma}=\argmin_{\Sigma\succeq 0}\max_{i,j}\sqrt{n_{ij}}|\Sigma_{ij}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{ij}|.

(11)

Similar to
[
16
]
, we introduce an additional variable
B\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
, choose a small
\varepsilon>0
and solve

(\widetilde{\Sigma},\widehat{B})=\argmin_{\Sigma\succeq\varepsilon I,B=\Sigma-\widehat{\Sigma}}\max_{i,j}\sqrt{n_{ij}}|B_{ij}|.

Consider the augmented Lagrangian function of the objective above:

\begin{split}&f(\Sigma,B,\Lambda)\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\max_{i,j}\sqrt{n_{ij}}|B_{ij}|-\langle\Lambda,\Sigma-B-\widehat{\Sigma}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\mu}\|\Sigma-B-\widehat{\Sigma}\|_{F}^{2},\end{split}

where
\Lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
is the Lagrangian variable, and
\mu
is a penalty parameter. Then the ADMM algorithm
[
6
]
takes the following steps at the
i
th iteration:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{(i+1)}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{\Sigma\succeq\varepsilon I}f(\Sigma,B^{(i)},\Lambda^{(i)}),

(12)

\displaystyle B^{(i+1)}=

\displaystyle\argmin_{B}f(\Sigma^{(i+1)},B,\Lambda^{(i)}),

(13)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(i+1)}=

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(i)}-\frac{\Sigma^{(i+1)}-B^{(i+1)}-\widehat{\Sigma}}{\mu}.

(14)

Now we discuss how to solve (
12
) and (
13
). One can show that

\begin{split}\Sigma^{(i+1)}=&\argmin_{\Sigma\succeq\varepsilon I}f(\Sigma,B^{(i)},\Lambda^{(i)})\\
=&\argmin_{\Sigma\succeq\varepsilon I}\|\Sigma-B^{(i)}-\mu\Lambda^{(i)}-\widehat{\Sigma}\|_{F}^{2}\\
=&\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}^{++}_{\varepsilon}}(B^{(i)}+\mu\Lambda^{(i)}+\widehat{\Sigma}),\end{split}

where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}^{++}_{\varepsilon}}(\cdot)
is the projection operator upon
\mathcal{S}^{++}_{\varepsilon}=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:A=A^{\top},\lambda_{\min}(A)\geq\varepsilon\}
, w.r.t. the Frobenius norm. We can perform an eigenvalue thresholding to find this projection.

While for
B^{(i+1)}
, the following lemma shows how to solve (
13
):

Lemma 1
.

Let
\omega\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
with
\omega_{ij}=\sqrt{n_{ij}}
. The solution for (
13
) is:

B^{(i+1)}=\Sigma^{(i+1)}-\mu\Lambda^{(i)}-\widehat{\Sigma}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{\omega^{-1},1}(\frac{\mu}{2})}(\Sigma^{(i+1)}-\mu\Lambda^{(i)}-\widehat{\Sigma}),

where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{\omega^{-1},1}(\frac{\mu}{2})}(\cdot)
is the projection operator upon the weighted
\ell_{1}
ball

\mathbb{B}_{\omega^{-1},1}(\frac{\mu}{2})=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:\sum_{i,j}\omega^{-1}_{ij}|A_{ij}|\leq\frac{\mu}{2}\},

w.r.t. the Frobenius norm.

Proof of Lemma
1
.

Let
A=\Sigma^{(i+1)}-\mu\Lambda^{(i)}-\widehat{\Sigma}
. First we can write

B^{(i+1)}=\argmin_{B}\|B-A\|_{F}^{2}+\mu\|\omega\circ B\|_{\infty}.

Due to the convexity of
g(B)=\|B-A\|_{F}^{2}+\mu\|\omega\circ B\|_{\infty}
, it suffices to show that
0_{p\times p}
is one sub-gradient of
g(B)
at
\widehat{B}:=A-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{\omega^{-1},1}(\frac{\mu}{2})}(A)
.

Let
\delta=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{\omega^{-1},1}(\mu/2)}(A)
, and
z=\frac{2}{\mu}\delta
. It is straightforward to verify that

(\nabla_{B}\|B-A\|_{F}^{2})_{B=\widehat{B}}+\mu z=0
, thus we only need to show that
z
is a sub-gradient of
\|\omega\circ B\|_{\infty}
at
B=\widehat{B}
, that is, (i)
\|\omega^{-1}\circ z\|_{1}\leq 1
and (ii)
\langle\widehat{B},z\rangle=\|\omega\circ\widehat{B}\|_{\infty}
.

First note that
\|\omega^{-1}\circ\delta\|_{1}<\frac{\mu}{2}
if and only if
\|\omega^{-1}\circ A\|_{1}<\frac{\mu}{2}
,
\delta=A
and
\widehat{B}=0
, which implies
\|\omega^{-1}\circ z\|_{1}<1
and
\langle\widehat{B},z\rangle=\|\omega\circ\widehat{B}\|_{\infty}=0
. Now we focus on the case where
\|\omega^{-1}\circ\delta\|_{1}=\frac{\mu}{2}
. In this case, we can still easily verify (i) since
\|\omega^{-1}\circ z\|_{1}=\frac{2}{\mu}\|\delta\|_{\omega^{-1},1}=1
. While for (ii), note that by established results for projection on weighted
\ell_{1}
ball
[
41
]
, there exists
c>0
such that
\delta_{ij}=\max\{|A_{ij}|-c\omega_{ij}^{-1},0\}\mathrm{sgn}(A_{ij}).
Then one can show that
\|\omega\circ\widehat{B}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i,j}\omega_{ij}|A_{ij}-\delta_{ij}|=c
. Furthermore,

z_{ij}>0
implies
A_{ij}>c\omega_{ij}^{-1}
, and
z_{ij}<0
implies
A_{ij}<-c\omega_{ij}^{-1}
. Hence we have

\begin{split}\langle\widehat{B},z\rangle=&\sum_{i,j}z_{ij}c\omega_{ij}^{-1}\mathrm{sgn}(A_{ij})\\
=&\sum_{i,j}c\omega_{ij}^{-1}|z_{ij}|\\
=&c\\
=&\|\omega\circ\widehat{B}\|_{\infty}.\end{split}

∎

Hence solving for
B^{(i+1)}
only requires the projection upon a weighted
\ell_{1}
ball, and this projection can be done by applying the algorithm proposed in
[
41
]
. We summarize the full ADMM algorithm for solving (
1
) in Algorithm
2
.

Input:
\widehat{\Sigma}
,
n_{ij},1\leq i,j\leq p
,
\varepsilon>0
, penalty parameter
\mu>0
, initial values
B_{0}
,
\Lambda_{0}

\omega_{ij}=\sqrt{n_{ij}}
;

1

for

i=0,1,\dots,K

do

2

\Sigma^{(i+1)}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{S}^{++}_{\varepsilon}}(B^{(i)}+\widehat{\Sigma}+\mu\Lambda^{(i)})
;

3

A=\Sigma^{(i+1)}-\mu\Lambda^{(i)}-\widehat{\Sigma}
;

4

B^{(i+1)}=A-\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{B}_{\omega,1}(\mu/2)}(A)
;

5

\Lambda^{(i+1)}=\Lambda^{(i)}-\frac{\Sigma^{(i+1)}-B^{(i+1)}-\widehat{\Sigma}}{\mu}
;

6

end for

7

Output:
\Sigma^{(K+1)}
.

Algorithm 2

ADMM for weighted
\ell_{\infty}
norm projection on positive semi-definite cone

Appendix B
Additional Theoretical Results and Discussion for Neighborhood Regression

Here, we first restate our neighborhood recovery guarantee (Theorem 1 in the main paper), and discuss the consequences of this result.
The neighborhood lasso estimator defined in (
2
) for each node
a
is:

\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}=\argmin_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\theta-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{a,:}\theta+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\theta_{j}|,

(15)

Assumption 7

(Mutual incoherence condition)
.

The population covariance
\Sigma^{*}
satisfies
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Sigma^{*}_{(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a})^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq 1-\gamma
for some
0<\gamma\leq 1
.

Theorem 6

(Neighborhood Selection Consistency)
.

Consider the model setting described in Section 1.1 and the estimator
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}
defined in (
2
).
Suppose Assumption
7
holds, and the tuning parameters
\lambda^{(a)}_{j}
’s in (
2
) satisfy
\lambda^{(a)}_{j}\asymp\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}
. If
\gamma_{a}\leq(\frac{2-c\gamma}{2-\gamma})^{2}
for some constant
c>0
, and

\min_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}\geq\frac{C_{2}\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{2}_{\infty}\kappa_{2}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\left[(\kappa_{3}^{2}+1)d_{a}^{2}+\frac{(\gamma+4)^{2}(\kappa_{1}+1)^{2}}{(\theta^{(a)}_{\min})^{2}}\right]\log p,

(16)

then
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{a}=\{j:\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}\neq 0\}=\mathcal{N}_{a}
with probability at least
1-p^{-c}
for some absolute constants
c,C_{1},C_{2}>0
.

Remark 7
.

Theorem
6
suggests that, even when many pairs of nodes are only measured few times together, as long as the tuning parameters are chosen carefully w.r.t. the pairwise sample sizes, we are still able to recover the neighborhood of node
a
. In particular, we only need to collect sufficient samples for a pair of nodes if at least one node in this pair is a neighbor of
a
. This is not a trivial result, since the estimator (
2
) has to perform a selection from its neighbors and a large number of non-neighbors which are not measured well.

For ease of presentation, here we have assumed that
n_{j,k}>0,\forall j,k
so that
\lambda^{(a)}_{k}
is well defined. However, with a slight modification of our proof, the
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{1}
error bounds in Theorem
6
would still hold even if
n_{j,k}=0
for some
j,k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}
, and if we simply define
\lambda^{(a)}_{j}\asymp\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\max\limits\{1,\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}\}}}
.

Remark 8

(Effect of differing sample sizes)
.

Theorem
6
also imposes a constraint on
\gamma_{a}
, the sample size ratio between the most well measured non-neighbor and the worst measured neighbor of
a
. The is due to that when the sample sizes for the non-neighbors are all much larger than the neighbors (large
\gamma_{a}
), the neighbors would suffer from much stronger regularization than non-neighbors.

Remark 9

(Comparison with the literature)
.

When all pairwise sample sizes are all equal,
\gamma_{a}=1
and this requirement becomes
\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{j,k}\geq C(\Sigma^{*})d_{a}^{2}\log p
, which is similar to the standard sample size requirement for neighborhood lasso in
[
37
,
49
]
, except for an additional factor
d_{a}
. This additional factor is due to technical challenges brought by non-simultaneous measurements of all variables.

Theorem 7
.

If the tuning parameters
\lambda_{j}
’s in the neighborhood regression estimator satisfy

\lambda^{(a)}_{j}\asymp\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}\geq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}(\gamma_{a}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}d_{a}^{2}\log p,

then with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}d_{a}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{\lambda^{(a)}_{j},1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{2}_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|^{2}_{1}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}.

One key sample size quantity in Theorem
7
is
\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}
, which is illustrated in Figure
5
. With appropriately chosen regularization parameters
\lambda_{j}^{(a)}
that reflect the corresponding sample sizes for each node, Theorem
7
shows that when
\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}
is sufficiently large, we can establish the both
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{1}
consistency of the neighborhood lasso estimator
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}
.
The number of node pairs involved is
d_{a}p
, much smaller than the total number of node pairs
p^{2}
when the degree of node
a
is small.

Figure 5:
Illustration of node pairs whose pairwise sample sizes play key roles in the estimation error rate
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}
,
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}
.

Appendix C
GI-JOE (FDR) Algorithm

We summarize our full GI-JOE (FDR) procedure in Algorithm
3
. It calls the edge-wise inference algorithm for each node pair
(a,b)
for
1\leq a<b\leq p
.

Input
: Data set
\{x_{i,V_{i}}:V_{i}\subset[p]\}_{i=1}^{n}
, pairwise sample sizes
\{n_{j,k}\}_{j,k=1}^{p}
, significance level
\alpha

for

1\leq a<b\leq p

do

1

Run Algorithm 1 (edge-wise inference) in the main paper for node pair
(a,b)
and obtain
p
-value
p_{a,b}
Sort the
p
-values of
m:=\frac{p(p-1)}{2}
pairs as
\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{1}}\leq\dots\leq\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{m}}
.

Let
k=m
,
t_{p}=(2\log m-2\log\log m)^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

while

\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{k}}\geq 2(1-\Phi(t_{p}))
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{k}}>\frac{\alpha k}{m}

do

2

k=k-1
if

\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{k}}<2(1-\Phi(t_{p}))

then

3

k=\argmax_{l}\boldsymbol{p}_{i_{l}}\leq 2(1-\Phi(\sqrt{2\log m})
.

Output
: Set
\widetilde{E}
of node pairs
(a,b)
associated with the
k
smallest
p
-values.

Algorithm 3

GI-JOE: FDR control

Recall that in the main paper, we have presented a theoretical guarantee for our GI-JOE (FDR) approach, under Assumptions
5
and
6
.
Here, we specifically give an example for when Assumption
6
holds in the simultaneous measurement setting.

Proposition 4
.

Consider the special case where all
p
variables are observed simultaneously with
n
samples, and let
\Omega^{*}=\mathrm{diag}(\Theta^{*})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Theta^{*}\mathrm{diag}(\Theta^{*})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
be the normalized precision matrix. Then Assumption
6
can be implied by the following conditions: there exists
0<\rho_{0}<1
such that

1.

for
1\leq j\leq p
,
|\{k:|\Omega_{j,k}|>\rho_{0}\}|\leq C
for some constant
C
;

2.

the maximum degree of the graph satisfies
d\ll p^{\frac{1-\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}}(\log p)^{\frac{\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}-\frac{1}{4}}(\log\log p)^{-\frac{1}{4}}
.

Proposition
4
suggests that, for the special setting where all variables are simultaneously observed, Assumption
6
can be directly implied if (i) each node only has constant number of strongly connected neighbors; (ii) a polynomial of the maximum degree of the graph is much smaller than the total number of variables
p
. Unfortunately, in the more general setting considered in this paper, it is extremely hard to simplify
\rho_{n}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
due to the complicated structures induced by the erose measurements. However, as we will see in our empirical results, for many of the erose measurement settings and graph types where the sample size assumptions may even be violated, the FDP is still controlled, which may suggest that our Assumption
6
is not overly stringent.

Appendix D
Confidence Intervals for Each Edge

In this section, we extend our GI-JOE edge-wise testing method to construct confidence intervals for each edge. Specifically, we propose confidence intervals both for
\theta^{(a)*}_{b}=-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
and
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
, where the former is a simple extension of the edge-wise testing algorithm, while the latter requires new method and normal approximation theory.

For
\theta^{(a)*}_{b}=-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
, we consider the following confidence interval:

\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\alpha}^{a,b}=[\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b),\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)].

(17)

The following theorem shows the validity of the confidence interval (
17
), a direct consequence of Theorem
3
.

Theorem 8

(Coverage and Width of Confidence Intervals)
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4
, the confidence interval
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}
defined in (
17
) satisfies

\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\theta^{(a)*}_{b}\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\alpha}^{a,b})=1-\alpha
,
|\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\alpha}^{a,b}|\overset{p}{\rightarrow}2z_{\alpha/2}\sigma_{n}(a,b)
, where
|\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\alpha}^{a,b}|
is the width of the confidence interval.

Although the inference target
\theta^{(a)*}_{b}
covered by the confidence interval above shares the same non-zero pattern as
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
, sometimes it might be of interest to directly construct a confidence interval for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
instead of
-\frac{\Theta_{a,b}^{*}}{\Theta_{a,a}^{*}}
. We first consider the case where
a\neq b
, and discuss our confidence interval for each diagonal element
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}
later. To achieve this purpose, here we propose the following test statistic for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
when
a\neq b
. Noting that
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}=(\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,:}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Sigma^{*}\frac{\Theta^{*}_{:,a}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}})^{-1}
, we can estimate
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}
by
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}:=(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)})^{-1}
, where
\widehat{\Sigma}
is the entry-wise estimate of the covariance matrix, and
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
serves as an estimate for
\frac{\Theta^{*}_{:,a}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
, satisfying
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{a}=1
and
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{\backslash a}=-\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{\backslash a}
. Then we consider the test statistic

\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}:=-\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b},

(18)

with
\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}
being the debiased neighborhood lasso estimate, and construct a confidence interval centered around it. Similar to the proof of Theorem
2
, some computations can show that the asymptotic variance of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}
is
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\times_{3}\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
, and hence we estimate it by

\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b):=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}\times_{1}\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}\times_{2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}\times_{3}\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}\times_{4}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:},

(19)

where
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}
is an estimator for
\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}
:

(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,k^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,j^{\prime}})\frac{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
; and
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}
are appropriately constructed estimators with several options.

Choices of
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}

For
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}
, note that
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
defined by
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{a}=1
and
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{\backslash a}=-\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{\backslash a}
can serve as an estimate for
\frac{1}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
. Hence we can consider
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}=\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
, where
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}=(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)})^{-1}
was defined in the beginning of this section. One can also substitute the entry-wise estimate
\widehat{\Sigma}
in
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
by
\widetilde{\Sigma}
, the projected positive semi-definite estimate for
\Sigma^{*}
, and obtain
\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,a}=(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)})^{-1}
. As we will show in Lemma
2
, both
\widehat{\Sigma}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}
satisfy the same entry-wise error bound, and hence using either of the following two estimates

\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}=\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a},\text{ or }\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}=\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,a}

(20)

would lead to the same estimation error bound for the variance. While for
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}
, one can either construct
it by another node-wise regression for node
b
upon the other
p-1

nodes, or one can make use of the estimators for
\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}
previously constructed in the debiasing step. For computational efficiency, here we focus on the latter approach. In particular, note that
\Theta^{*}_{:,b}=\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
, and hence one can estimate
\Theta^{*}_{:,b}
by

\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}=\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(1)}+\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)},

(21)

where
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(1)}
is an estimate for
\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)}
is an estimate for
\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
.
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(1)}
can be set as
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
or
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
previously defined in Section 3.1 of the main paper:

\begin{split}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}=&(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{b,:}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)})^{-1},\,\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}=\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)},\\
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}=&(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)})^{-1},\,\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}=\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)},\end{split}

where
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)}
was based on the node-wise regression of node
b
upon
[p]\backslash\{a,b\}
, defined in the main paper. One can also substitute
\widetilde{\Sigma}
by
\widehat{\Sigma}
.
While for estimating
\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
, since
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
is an estimate for
\frac{1}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta_{:,a}
,
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}
is an estimate for
-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
,
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)}
can be set as

\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)}=-\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)},\text{ or }\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)}=-\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}.

(22)

Similar to estimating
\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
, these options above for estimating
\Theta^{*}_{:,b}
use either
\widehat{\Sigma}
or
\widetilde{\Sigma}
, and can satisfy the same statistical error bounds due to the same entry-wise error guarantee of
\widehat{\Sigma}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}
in Lemma
2
.

Then when
a\neq b
, we construct our confidence interval for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
by

\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,b}=[\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,b),\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}+z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)].

(23)

We can also provide inference for each diagonal element
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}
based on test statistic
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
. Let
\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,a)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}\times_{1}\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}\times_{2}\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}\times_{3}\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}\times_{4}\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}
. Then we can construct the confidence interval for
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
as follows:

\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,a}=[\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,a),\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}+z_{\alpha/2}\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,a)].

(24)

Note here that all extra computations needed for the inference of
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
are elementary and basically free.

In the following, we present theoretical guarantees to show the asymptotic validity of these confidence intervals under similar conditions to Assumptions
1
-
4
. Define
S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)=S_{1}(a,b)\cup\{(a,a)\}
for
a\neq b
and
S_{1}^{\prime}(a,a)=\{(j,k):j\text{ or }k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\}
;
S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)=(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b})\cup(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a})
for
a\neq b
and
S_{2}^{\prime}(a,a)=\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
. Let
n_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}
and
n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}
for arbitrary
a,\,b\in[p]
. We also define
\gamma_{a}^{(a)}=\gamma_{a}
, and key constant terms

\begin{split}C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)=\begin{cases}\frac{C\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}(\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|)}{\lambda_{\min}(\Theta^{*})\min_{(j,k)\in S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)}|\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{b,k}+\Theta^{*}_{a,k}\Theta^{*}_{b,j}|},&a\neq b;\\
\frac{C\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}|\Theta^{*}_{j,a}|^{2}},&a=b.\end{cases}\end{split}

(25)

Assumption 8
.

\displaystyle n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}\geq C\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{4}+\gamma_{a}+\gamma_{b}^{(a)})(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2}\log p.

Remark 10

(
n_{1}^{(a,b)}=n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}
)
.

Note that since
n_{a,a}=\sum_{i}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{a\in V_{i}\}}\geq\sum_{i}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{a,j\in V_{i}\}}=n_{a,j}
for all
j
, we immediately have that
n_{a,a}\geq\min_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\mathcal{N}_{b}^{(a)}}n_{a,j}=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}(a,b)}n_{j,k}
. Therefore,
n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}=n_{1}^{(a,b)}
always holds, and it is equivalent to change
n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}
in Assumption
8
,
10
, and
11
to
n_{1}^{(a,b)}
.

Assumption 9
.

C_{\varepsilon}(\Sigma^{*})(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\varepsilon}=o(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})
for some constant
\varepsilon>0
, where
C_{\varepsilon}(\Sigma^{*})=\left(\frac{C(1+2/\epsilon)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\right)^{2+\epsilon}
.

Assumption 10
.

n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}\gg C^{\prime 2}(\Theta^{*};a,b)(\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{4}+\gamma_{a}+\gamma^{(a)}_{b})[(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p]^{2}\frac{n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}},

(26)

where
C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)
is defined in (
25
).

Assumption 11
.

n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}\gg C^{\prime 4}(\Theta^{*};a,b)(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2}\log p\left(\frac{n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}\right)^{2},

where
C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)
is defined in (
25
).

Theorem 9
.

Consider the inference for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
for any nodes
a,\,b\in[p]
. Suppose we construct the confidence interval
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,b}
as in (
23
) if
a\neq b
, and as in (
24
) if
a=b
. Then with appropriately chosen tuning parameters as specified in Theorem
2
, if Assumptions
8
-
11
hold, the confidence interval
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,b}
satisfies
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\Theta^{*}_{a,b}\in\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,b})=1-\alpha
.

The detailed assumptions and proof of Theorem
9
can be found in Section
I.5.3
.
Assumptions
8
-
11
are very similar to Assumptions
1
-
4
, the sample size conditions required for the valid inference of
\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
. One main difference is that Assumptions
9
-
11
consider a slightly different sample size quantity
n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}
, which by Proposition
1
is larger than
n_{2}^{(a,b)}
. Therefore, under similar assumptions and without additional computations, our procedure can also lead to an asymptotically valid confidence interval for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
.

Now we present some simulation results that validate our confidence intervals for the entries of the precision matrix. In particular, for
\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)
defined in (
19
), we choose
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,a}=\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
,
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}
as in (
21
) with
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(1)}=(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)})^{-1}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)}
, and
\widehat{\Theta}_{:,b}^{(2)}=-\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
. That is, we use
\widehat{\Sigma}
instead of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
throughout for our variance estimation.
We follow the same pairwise measurement set-up as in Section 5.1 of the main paper, and chain graphs, multi-star graphs with three stars, and Erdős–Rényi graphs are considered. Since the minimum pairwise sample size conditions in Assumptions
8
-
11
imposed on edge sets
S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)
and
S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)
can be implied by or equivalent to the same sample size conditions on
S_{1}(a,b)
and
S_{2}(a,b)
, here we still set
n_{j,k}=n1
if
(j,k)\in S_{1}(a,b)\backslash S_{2}(a,b)
, and set
n_{j,k}=n2
if
(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)
. We provide confidence intervals for
\Theta^{*}_{2,4}=0
and
\Theta^{*}_{2,3}\neq 0
with different signal strength. The number of nodes is fixed as
p=200
, and
n2/n1=1.5
. Figure
6
presents the coverage result of our confidence interval over
1000
replicates, where the error bars represent
95\%
confidence interval for the coverage rate. The dashed line is the target coverage, and we can see that the coverage of our confidence interval is close to the target rate
95\%
.

Figure 6:
Average coverage of the proposed confidence intervals for
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
over
1000
replicates. Error bars represent
95\%
confidence interval for the coverage rate, and the dashed line is the target coverage. Each panel considers one signal strength
\sqrt{n_{2}}\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=0,2.2,4.5
.

Appendix E
Numerical Details

E.1
Tuning Parameter Selection in the Simulations on Edge-wise Inference

Our estimators
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}^{(a)}
depend on tuning parameters
\lambda^{(a)}_{j}
and
\lambda^{(a,b)}_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq p
. Based on the theoretical scaling, we set
\lambda^{(a)}_{j}=C_{1}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}
, and
\lambda^{(a,b)}_{j}=C_{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}
, where
C_{1},\,C_{2}
are chosen based on neighborhood selection stability
[
35
]
over
20
random subsamples for each corresponding neighborhood, with the stability threshold set as
0.05
, as suggested by
[
35
]
. For each random subsample, each sample is included with probability
0.8
independently. The candidates for the tuning constant
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are set as a geometric sequence of length
20
, spaced between
[C_{1,\max}/10,C_{1,\max}]
and
[C_{2,\max}/10,C_{2,\max}]
.
C_{1,\max},\,C_{2,\max}
are chosen as the regularization scaling that leads to a zero neighborhood regression estimate. For each experimental setting, 200 or 1000 independent replicates are run, but we only perform stability selection for one replicate and use the same tuning parameter for all replicates.

E.2
Implementation Details in Graph Selection Comparison

Estimation Methods:

For baseline plug-in type methods, we directly plug in the covariance matrix estimate
\widetilde{\Sigma}
(the positive semi-definite matrix solved by the ADMM algorithm) into the graphical lasso, neighborhood lasso, and CLIME algorithms to estimate the graph structure. This is slightly different from the original plug-in type methods where unbiased estimate
\widehat{\Sigma}
is in use. We choose
\widetilde{\Sigma}
to ensure convexity or algorithmic stability. We choose the tuning parameters for all methods using stability selection.
20
random subsamplings are used for Nlasso, Glasso, Nlasso-JOE, and
10
random subsamplings are used for CLIME to save computational time. For each random subsampling, each sample is included with probability
0.8
, and the stability threshold is
0.05
.

Implementation Details of Inference Methods:

The inference methods include GI-JOE (Holm), GI-JOE (FDR), DB-Glasso (Holm), and DB-Glasso (FDR). For GI-JOE (Holm) and GI-JOE (FDR), the tuning parameters in the neighborhood regression problems are set as
\lambda_{j}^{(a)},\lambda_{j}^{(a,b)}=C\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}
, and the constant
C
is the same over the whole graph. We choose
C
by stability selection on the graph, with
20
random subsampling and stability threshold
0.05
. Same as the tuning procedure for edge-wise simulations, for each random subsampling, each sample is included with probability
0.8
independently. The candidate set for
C
is also a geometric sequence spaced between
[C_{\max}/10,C_{\max}]
, where
C_{\max}
is the smallest constant that leads to empty graph estimate. this data-drive tuning procedure was done for each random replicate in the experiments. For DB-Glasso (Holm) and DB-Glasso (FDR), we first plug in
\widetilde{\Sigma}
into graphical lasso to obtain an estimate
\widehat{\Theta}
of the precision matrix; then we compute the debiased graphical lasso statistic:

\widehat{T}=2\widehat{\Theta}-\widehat{\Theta}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\Theta}
.
The variance of each edge
(j,k)
is estimated by
\frac{1}{\min_{j,k}n_{j,k}}\widehat{\sigma}_{j,k}^{2}=\widehat{\Theta}_{j,j}\widehat{\Theta}_{k,k}+\widehat{\Theta}_{j,k}^{2}
, where we plug in the minimum pairwise sample size. Then we normalize the edge statistic to
\frac{\sqrt{\min_{j,k}n_{j,k}}\widehat{T}_{j,k}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{j,k}}
and compute its
p
-value
p_{j,k}=2(1-\Phi(\frac{\sqrt{\min_{j,k}n_{j,k}}|\widehat{T}_{j,k}|}{\widehat{\sigma}_{j,k}}))
. Then we add Holm’s correction and FDR control procedure on top of these edge-wise
p
-values, similar to what GI-JOE (Holm) and GI-JOE (FDR).

E.3
Testing against a Threshold

Suppose we would like to test the hypothesis
H_{0,(a,b)}:|\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}|\leq\epsilon
for some positive value
\epsilon
. We propose to find the
p
-value for
H_{0,(a,b)}
as follows:

p_{a,b}=\min\{1,2(1-\Phi(\frac{|\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}|-\epsilon}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}))\}
. Now we show that the validity of this
p
-value is directly implied by our current theoretical results. Note that under
H_{0,(a,b)}
, for any
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\frac{|\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}|-\epsilon}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}>t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\frac{|\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}|-|\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}|}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}>t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\frac{|\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}|}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}>t)\rightarrow 2(1-\Phi(t)),

where the last line is due to Theorem 3 in the main paper. After getting the
p
-values for all node pairs, we can further apply the Holm’s correction and the FDR control procedure upon these
\frac{p(p-1)}{2}

p
-values.

Appendix F
Comparison between Different Variance Estimates for Edge-wise Inference

As mentioned in Section 3.1 of the main paper, there are different potential estimates for
\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}
, including
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
and
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
defined as follows:

\begin{split}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}=&(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{b,:}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)})^{-1},\,\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}=\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)},\\
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}=&(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)})^{-1},\,\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}=\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,b)}.\end{split}

One may estimate the variance of the edge-wise statistic by

\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{n}\times_{1}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{2}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\times_{3}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{4}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)},

(27)

or

\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{n}\times_{1}\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{2}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\times_{3}\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{4}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}.

(28)

Both estimators satisfy the same theoretical error bounds, but we specifically recommend practitioners to use (
28
), since it controls the Type I error rate better. In particular, we report the variance estimation results of both (
27
) and (
28
) in our edge-wise inference simulations in Figure
7
. We observe that both estimates tend to underestimate the true variance, while (
28
) is slightly better than (
27
). As a consequence, constructing the test statistic with variance estimate in (
28
) leads to better Type I error control, as demonstrated in Figure
8
. We have also empirically investigated using
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
for the debiasing step, while it turns out that the combination that uses
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
for debiasing and
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}
for variance estimate results in the best type I error control.

Figure 7:
Variance estimation results of
1000
replicates in the edge-wise inference simulations when
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=0
. The top figure presents
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)/\sigma_{n}(a,b)
when
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)
is chosen as in (
27
), while the bottom figure presents the ratio when
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)
is as in (
28
). The red line represents the mean of
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)/\sigma_{n}(a,b)
over
1000
replicates. We can see that both variance estimates tend to be smaller than the true variance (
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)/\sigma_{n}(a,b)<1
most of the time), choosing
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)
as in (
28
) leads to slightly better estimate, especially for chain graphs.

Figure 8:
Type I error rates in edge-wise inference simulations. The left figure is when the test statistics is constructed with the variance estimate in (
27
), and the right figure is using (
28
). Hence we recommend the practitioners to use (
28
) for better type I error control.

Appendix G
Additional Empirical Results

In this section, we present some additional empirical results, including the detailed true positive rates (TPR), true negative rates (TNR), true discovery rate (TDR) and F1 scores in the graph selection studies (Table
3
-
16
). Specifically, TPR is the ratio of selected true edges and total number of true edges; TNR is the ratio of unselected nonedges and total number of nonedges; TDR is the ratio of selected true edges and selected edges. In summary, estimation methods tend to be much more liberal (higher TPR but lower TDR) while inference methods are more conservative (higher TDR but lower TPR). The debiased graphical lasso methods with minimum sample size are often too conservative and select no edge at all, hence their TDR are sometimes NA.

Appendix H
The Graphs and Measurement Patterns in Real Data-inspired Simulations

In Section 5.3 of the main paper, we present simulation results (i) when the underlying graph for data generation is estimated from a real calcium imaging data set; and (ii) when the measurement patterns are the same as the ones in two single-cell RNA sequencing data sets. We present here the estimated graph structure from neuroscience and the measurement patterns from gene expression data in Figure
9
and Figure
10
.

Figure 9:
The estimated graph from the calcium imaging data set
[
32
]
. We generate data from this graph in our first set of simulations presented in Section 5.3.

(a)

Observational patterns

(b)

Pairwise sample sizes

Figure 10:
Observational patterns and pairwise sample sizes of two real scRNA-seq data sets
[
12
,
13
]
, including top 200 genes with the highest variances. The pairwise sample sizes range from 0 to 1018 (
chu
data, left) and from 5 to 366 (
darmanis
, right). These are the observational patterns used in our second set of simulations presented in Section 5.3.

Generation of scale-free and small-world graphs:

The scale-free graph is generated from the Barabasi-Albert model with
200
nodes, and for each new node sequentially added to the model, one edge between it and the existing nodes is randomly created. The small-world graph is generated from the Watts-Strogatz model with degree
2
and rewiring probability
0.5
.

Appendix I
Proofs

For convenience, here we restate some of our key notations presented in the main paper. Recall the definition of node pair index sets
S_{1}(a,b)
and
S_{2}(a,b)
:

\begin{split}S_{1}(a,b)=&\{(j,k):j\text{ or }k\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}^{(a)}\},\\
S_{2}(a,b)=&\{(j,k):\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}\neq 0\},\end{split}

(29)

and sample size quantities
n_{1}(a,b)=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}(a,b)}n_{j,k}
,
n_{2}(a,b)=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}n_{j,k}
.

I.1
Proof of Theorem
7

Proof of Theorem
7
.

By the definition of
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}
, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{a,:}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}|\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\theta^{(a)*\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)*}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{a,:}\theta^{(a)*}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|,\end{split}

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{:,a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}\theta^{(a)*})^{\top}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}(|\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|-|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{\infty}(\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}+1)|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}(|\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|-|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}|)

(30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}(|\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|-|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}|).

(31)

The following lemma provides an upper bound for
\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{\infty}
:

Lemma 2

(Entry-wise error bounds for
\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}
)
.

With probability at least
1-p^{-c}
, for
1\leq i,j\leq p
,

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Sigma}_{i,j}-\Sigma^{*}_{i,j}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{i,j}}},

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{i,j}-\Sigma^{*}_{i,j}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{i,j}}}.

Lemma
2
suggests that

\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{\infty}(\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}+1)\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\leq\frac{\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}{2}

with high probability. Together with (
31
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\lambda_{j}}{2}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|+\lambda_{j}^{(a)}(|\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|-|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\frac{3}{2}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|-\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|,

where
\mathcal{N}_{a}=\{j\neq a:\Theta^{*}_{j,a}\neq 0\}=\{j:\theta^{(a)*}_{j}\neq 0\}
and
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}=\{a\}\cup\mathcal{N}_{a}
. By construction,
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is positive semi-definite, which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|\leq 3\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\widehat{\theta}_{j}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}|.

(32)

Our next step is to show a lower bound of the L.H.S. of the inequality above. Let
\Delta=\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}
. First note that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\Delta\geq\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})-\frac{1}{2}|\Delta^{\top}(\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Delta|,

where the latter term can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle|\Delta^{\top}(\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Delta|\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\left|\frac{\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}}{\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}\right||\Delta_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\right)\|\Delta\|_{1}\max\limits_{j,k}\left|\frac{\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}}{\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}{2\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\right)\|\Delta\|_{1},

where the last line is due to Lemma
2
. By (
32
),

\begin{split}\|\Delta\|_{1}=&\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}|\Delta_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}|\Delta_{j}|\\
\leq&\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}|\Delta_{j}|+\frac{1}{\min\limits_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}}\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\Delta_{j}|\\
\leq&\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}|\Delta_{j}|+\frac{3\max\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}}{\min\limits_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}|\Delta_{j}|\\
\leq&\left(3\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1\right)\sqrt{d_{a}}\|\Delta\|_{2},\end{split}

(33)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\Delta_{j}|\leq

\displaystyle 4\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\Delta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\max\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}\sqrt{d_{a}}\|\Delta\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\|\Delta\|_{2}.

Hence we can further bound the term above as follows:

\displaystyle|\Delta^{\top}(\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Delta|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}^{2}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\left(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1\right)\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}{2}\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2},

where the last line is due to our condition that

\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}\geq C\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}(\gamma_{a}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}d_{a}^{2}\log p.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{6}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\Delta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j}\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\|\Delta\|_{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|_{2}\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}.

While for the
\ell_{1}
norm error, one can apply (
33
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\|\Delta\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}^{2}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}|\Delta_{j}|\leq

\displaystyle 4\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda_{j}^{(a)}|\Delta_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
6
.

We first would like to show that there is no false positive:
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{(\mathcal{N}_{a})^{c}}=0
, and the second step is to show no false negative in
\mathcal{N}_{a}
.
Let

\widetilde{\theta}=\arg\min_{\theta_{(\mathcal{N}_{a})^{c}=0}}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\theta-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{:,a}\theta+\|\lambda^{(a)}\circ\theta\|_{1},

and
\widetilde{\Delta}=\widetilde{\theta}-\theta^{*}
. Since
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is positive semi-definite,
\widetilde{\theta}
exists and satisfies the KKT condition,

\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}+\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},a}+\lambda^{(a)}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\circ\widetilde{Z}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}=0,

(34)

for some
\widetilde{Z}_{j}=\begin{cases}\mathrm{sgn}(\widetilde{\theta}_{j}),&j\in\mathcal{N}_{a},\widetilde{\theta}_{j}\neq 0,\\
\in[-1,1],&j\in\mathcal{N}_{a},\widetilde{\theta}_{j}=0.\end{cases}

We define
\widetilde{Z}_{j},j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}
to satisfy

\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}+\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},a}+\lambda^{(a)}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\circ\widetilde{Z}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}=0,

(35)

and we would like to show
\|\widetilde{Z}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, so that
\widetilde{\theta}
also satisfies the KKT condition of

\min_{\theta}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\theta-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{:,a}\theta+\|\lambda^{(a)}\circ\theta\|_{1}.

This would complete our proof for the no false positive statement.

By (
34
) and (
35
), we have that

\begin{split}\widetilde{Z}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}=&\frac{1}{\lambda^{(a)}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}}\circ\Big[\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\\
&-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}-\lambda^{(a)}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\circ\widetilde{Z}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}})\Big]\end{split}

In the following, we first bound
|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}|
for any
j\neq a
, then bound
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c},\mathcal{N}_{a}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}
.

Let
\widehat{W}=\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*}
, then we have

\begin{split}\left|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|=&\left|\Sigma^{*}_{j,a}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}+\widehat{W}_{j,a}-\widehat{W}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|\\
=&\left|\widehat{W}_{j,a}-\widehat{W}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|,\end{split}

(36)

where the last equation is due to the definition

\theta^{*}_{\backslash a}=(\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash a,\backslash a})^{-1}\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash a,a}.

By Proposition 1, with probability at least
1-p^{-c}
,

|\widehat{W}_{ij}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{ij}}}

holds for all
1\leq i,j\leq p
. Hence

\left|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,a}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,\mathcal{N}_{a}}\theta^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|\leq\|\widehat{W}_{j,\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\|_{\infty}(\kappa_{1}+1)\leq\frac{\gamma\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}{4}

(37)

for
j\neq a
.

On the other hand, for any
j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{c}_{a}
,

\begin{split}&\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\|(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}\|_{1}+d_{a}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\left\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}-\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}\right\|_{\infty}\\
&+d_{a}\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}\|_{1}\\
\leq&1-\gamma+\kappa_{3}d_{a}\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\\
&+Cd_{a}\left(\kappa_{2}+d_{a}\left\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\right\|_{\infty}\right)\\
&*\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{ij}}}\\
\leq&1-\frac{5\gamma}{6}+(\kappa_{3}+\frac{\gamma}{6\kappa_{2}})d_{a}\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}.\end{split}

(38)

Since

\begin{split}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq&{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\\
\leq&\kappa_{2}d_{a}\|\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|_{\infty}\\
\leq&C\kappa_{2}d_{a}\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{ij}}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2},\end{split}

where the last line is due to the sample size requirement in Theorem 1. Thus the following matrix expansion holds:

\begin{split}&(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\\
=&(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}+\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\\
=&\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}[(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}]^{k}(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\\
=&-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}J(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}.\end{split}

(39)

Here
J=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}[(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}]^{k}
and satisfies
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|J\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\infty}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\frac{1}{2})^{k}=2
. Hence we have

\begin{split}&\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{*}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\\
\leq&2\kappa_{2}^{2}\|\widehat{W}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|_{\infty}\\
\leq&2C\kappa_{2}^{2}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\max_{i,j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{ij}}}\\
\leq&\min\{\frac{\kappa_{2}}{d_{a}},\frac{\gamma}{6\kappa_{3}d_{a}}\}.\end{split}

(40)

Combining (
40
) and (
38
) implies

\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},\mathcal{N}_{a}})^{-1}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\mathcal{N}_{a},j}\|_{1}\leq 1-\frac{\gamma}{2}.

(41)

By (
36
), (
37
), (
41
), we have

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{Z}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{c}}\|_{\infty}\\
\leq&\frac{\gamma}{4}+(1-\frac{\gamma}{2})\frac{\max_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}{\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}_{a}}^{c}}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}}\\
\leq&c\gamma+(1-\frac{\gamma}{2})\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}\\
\leq&1,\end{split}

where the last inequality is due to the condition on
\gamma_{a}
.

Now we only need to upper bound
\|\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|_{\infty}
. Based on (
34
), (
37
) and (
40
), it is straightforward to see that

\|\Delta_{\mathcal{N}_{a}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\kappa_{2}(1+\frac{\gamma}{4})\max_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\lambda^{(a)}_{j}\leq\frac{\theta^{(a)}_{\min}}{2},

which implies that
\{j:\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}\neq 0\}=\mathcal{N}_{a}
and
\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j})=\mathrm{sgn}(\theta^{(a)*}_{j})=\mathrm{sgn}(\Theta^{*}_{ja})
.
∎

I.2
Proof of Theorem
2

We start by presenting the following lemma that guarantees the estimation performance of
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}
and hence helps establish our normal approximation result of the debiased neighborhood lasso. Define the sample size ratio for node
j
as

\gamma_{j}^{(a)}=\frac{\max\limits_{i\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)c}_{j}}\min\limits_{k}n_{i,k}}{\min\limits_{i\in\mathcal{N}^{(a)}_{j}}\min\limits_{k}n_{i,k}}.

Also recall the neighborhood lasso estimator used for debiasing in (
5
):

\begin{split}\widehat{\theta}^{(a,b)}=&\argmin_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{p},\theta_{a}=\theta_{b}=0}\frac{1}{2}\theta^{\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\theta-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{b,:}\theta+\sum_{k=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a,b)}_{k}|\theta_{k}|,\\
\widehat{\tau}^{(a,b)}=&(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{b,b}-\widetilde{\Sigma}_{b,:}\widehat{\theta}^{(a,b)})^{-1},\end{split}

(42)

Lemma 3
.

If the tuning parameters
\lambda^{(a,j)}_{k}
’s in (
5
) satisfy

\displaystyle\lambda^{(a,j)}_{k}\asymp

\displaystyle\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},

\displaystyle\min\limits_{i\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{i,k}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{2}_{\infty}(\gamma_{j}^{(a)}+\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2})}{\lambda_{\min\limits}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}d_{j}^{(a)2}\log p,

then with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{(a)}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}d^{(a)}_{j}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}},

where
d_{j}^{(a)}=|\{k:\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,k}\neq 0\}|
. Furthermore, the bounds above also hold when substituting
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}
with
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}
.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

By the definition of
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}
in (
6
):

\widehat{\Theta}_{j,:}^{(a)}=\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)},

(43)

we have

\begin{split}&\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|\\
\leq&|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|\\
&+|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|,\end{split}

(44)

where
\|\cdot\|
can either be
\ell_{2}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{2}
,
\ell_{1}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{1}
, or the weighted
\ell_{1}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}
defined as
\|\theta\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}=\sum_{k=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a,j)}_{k}|\theta_{k}|
, and

\displaystyle\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}

\displaystyle=(\Sigma^{*}_{j,j}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,\backslash\{a,j\}}(\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash\{a,j\},\backslash\{a,j\}})^{-1}\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash\{a,j\},j})^{-1},

\displaystyle\theta^{(a,j)*}

\displaystyle=(\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash\{a,j\},\backslash\{a,j\}})^{-1}\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash\{a,j\},j}.

For
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{2}
,
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}
, and
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}
,
we can bound them by the same arguments as the proof of Theorem
7
. The only difference lies that we substitute
\Sigma^{*}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}
by
\Sigma^{*}_{\backslash a,\backslash a}
and
\widetilde{\Sigma}_{\backslash a,\backslash a}
, and hence with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{2}\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},\\
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{j}}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)2}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},\\
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{2}}\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}.\end{split}

(45)

While for
|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|
, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|\leq

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}||(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|,

(46)

and by the definition of
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}
,

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|=

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,j}|+|(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,:})\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}|

\displaystyle+|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})_{j,:}\|_{\infty}(1+\|\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{\theta}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1})

\displaystyle+\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{2}.

Since
\|\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}+1=\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}
,
\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a,j)}-\theta^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}\leq\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}
due to (
45
) and the sample size condition in Lemma
3
, and
\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\geq\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})
, the error term
|(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|
can be further bounded by

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|

(47)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}

(48)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)2}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}

(49)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}},

(50)

where the last line is due to the fact that
\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}\lambda_{\max}(\Theta^{(a)*})\leq\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}\lambda_{\min}^{-1}(\Sigma^{*})
,
\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})
, and the sample size condition in Lemma
3
.
Plug in (
47
) into (
46
), one can show that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{2}\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},

and

\displaystyle|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}-|(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|]^{-1}\leq\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}.

Therefore, combining the bound above with (
45
) and (
44
), we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq&C\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}\\
&+\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},\end{split}

where the last line is due to that
\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})
and
\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\leq\lambda_{\min\limits}^{-1}(\Sigma^{*})
;

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\leq&C\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}\\
&+\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{(a)}}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)2}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{j}^{(a)}})d^{(a)}_{j}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},\end{split}

where we have applied the fact that
\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d^{(a)}_{j}}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}
. While for the
\|\cdot\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}
error, note that

\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\leq\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},

and hence we have

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}\leq&C\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Sigma^{*}_{j,:}\|_{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{3}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\frac{\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}\\
&+\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}\\
\leq&C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}^{2}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}.\end{split}

While for the corresponding bounds for
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}
, we only need to show that
|(\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|
satisfies similar bounds to the ones we showed for
|(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|
. Specifically,

\begin{split}|(\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|=&\left|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)\top}\widetilde{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*\top}\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\right|\\
\leq&|2(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*})^{\top}\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}|+|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)\top}(\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}|\\
&+|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*})^{\top}\Sigma^{*}(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*})|.\end{split}

Since
\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}=e_{j}
, the
j
th canonical vector in
\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}_{j}=\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}_{j}=1
, we know that the first term above is zero. On the other hand, Assumption
1
and (
45
) suggest that
\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}\|_{1}\leq\|\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}=\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}
, and suggest the following bounds:

\begin{split}|(\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|\leq&\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}\sum_{k}\|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,:}-\Sigma^{*}_{k,:}\|_{\infty}|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}_{k}|\\
\leq&\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a,j)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}\\
&+C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}^{(a)}\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{j}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}},\end{split}

where we have applied the error bound for
\widetilde{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*}
in Lemma
2
and the condition on tuning parameters
\lambda^{(a,j)}
in the second line. The last line is due to the fact that
\|\overline{\theta}^{(a,j)*}\|_{1}=\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\leq\sqrt{d^{(a)}_{j}}\frac{\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}}{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j}}\leq\sqrt{d^{(a)}_{j}}\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}
. In addition, since
\|\Theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}\|\Theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{d_{j}^{(a)}}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Theta^{(a)*}}
, Assumption
1
can further imply
|(\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,j})^{-1}-(\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,j})^{-1}|\leq\frac{1}{2\Theta^{(a))*}_{j,j}}
. The rest of the arguments for bounding
\|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{2}
,
\|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{1}
,
\|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{j,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,:}\|_{\lambda^{(a,j)},1}
follow similarly.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2
.

First we start with some algebra to decompose
\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b}=\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
into terms
B
and
E
. Here we applied the fact that
\theta^{(a)*}_{\backslash a}=-\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
. By definition (
7
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}(\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{:,a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}(\widehat{\Sigma}\theta^{(a)*}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{:,a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})+\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:})(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a}

\displaystyle+\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a},

where the second line is due to that
(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*})_{\backslash a}=e_{b}^{\top}
, and the third line holds since
\widehat{\Sigma}\theta^{(a)*}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{:,a}=-\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}\widehat{\Sigma}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
, and
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}=\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}e_{a}=0
.
Let

B=(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})+\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:})(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a},

(51)

and
E=\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
. We will show an upper bound for
|B|
and a normal approximation for
E
.

I.2.1
Bounding the bias term
B

We first further decompose the bias term
B
and upper bound it by functions of estimation errors
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}
and
\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}
.
One can show that

\displaystyle\left|(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}(\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})+(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:})\Sigma^{*}(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})

\displaystyle+(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:})(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\|\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{N}_{b}^{(a)},:}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{\overline{N}_{b}^{(a)},:}\|_{\infty}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\|\Sigma^{*}\|\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j,k}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}||\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}-\Theta^{*}_{b,j}||\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{k}-\theta^{(a)*}_{k}|.

Since

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}=\{j:\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\neq 0\}=

\displaystyle\{j:\Theta^{*}_{b,j}-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\neq 0\}

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle\{j:\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\neq 0\}\cup\{j\neq a:\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\neq 0\}=\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b},

we have
d_{b}^{(a)}\leq d_{a}+d_{b}+1
, and hence the sample size condition in Theorem
7
and Lemma
3
can be implied by Assumption
1
. By Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
and Theorem
7
, one can show that

\begin{split}&\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\|\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{N}_{b}^{(a)},:}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}^{(a)},:}\|_{\infty}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}\\
\leq&C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{j,k}},\\
&\|\Sigma^{*}\|\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}\\
\leq&C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{3}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}},\\
&\sum_{j,k}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}||\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}-\Theta^{*}_{b,j}||\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{k}-\theta^{(a)*}_{k}|\\
\leq&\frac{C\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{2}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\lambda^{(a)}_{k}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{k}-\theta^{(a)*}_{k}|\\
\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}\left(\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{j,k}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}.\end{split}

(52)

Here
