\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}=\frac{\lambda_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}
, and we have applied the fact that
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\geq\lambda_{\max\limits}^{-1}(\Sigma^{*})
,
\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{2}\leq\lambda^{-1}_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})
. Also noting that we have the condition

\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}\leq\frac{C\lambda^{2}_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}},

suggesting the upper bound for the third term in (
52
) can be dominated by the second term.
Hence we have

\displaystyle\left|(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}-\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\widehat{\Sigma})(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}.

While for the second term in (
51
), one can show that

\displaystyle|\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}(\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:})(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\|\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{:,\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}-\Sigma^{*}_{:,\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

|B|\leq C_{1}(\Theta^{*})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}},

with
C_{1}(\Theta^{*})=C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{b}^{(a)}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}
.

I.2.2
Normal approximation of term
E

Let
\delta^{(i)},N\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}={{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{j,k\in V_{i}\}}
and
N_{j,k}=n_{j,k}
. Then we can write
\widehat{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N
, where
\circ
represents Hadamard product and
\oslash
represents Hadamard division. With a little abuse of notation, when
N_{j,k}=0
, we let
\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{N_{j,k}}=0
.
To apply the central limit theorem and establish the normal approximation result, here we rewrite
E
as follows:

E=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Theta^{*-1}_{a,a}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}[(x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{*})\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N]\Theta^{*}_{:,a}.

Here we applied the fact that
(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma^{*}\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N)_{j,k}=\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}
as long as
N_{j,k}>0
, and
\min\limits_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}n_{j,k}>0
. Since the
n
terms inside the summation are independent, we can apply the central limit theorem. The variance
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)
can be calculated as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=

\displaystyle\Theta^{*-2}_{a,a}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}[(x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{*})\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N]\Theta^{*}_{:,a})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta^{*-2}_{a,a}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{j,k}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-1}x_{i,j}x_{i,k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta^{*-2}_{a,a}\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{k^{\prime},a}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}\delta^{(i)}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{3}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{:,a},

where we used the definition

\mathcal{T}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\mathrm{Cov}(x_{i,j}x_{i,k},x_{i,j^{\prime}}x_{i,k^{\prime}})=\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}}+\Sigma^{*}_{j,k^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{k,j^{\prime}}

on the third line, and
\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\mathcal{T}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
. In the following, we will verify the Lyapounov’s condition so that we can apply the central limit theorem.

Define
U,U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
by

\displaystyle U_{j,k}=\frac{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}}{2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}},

U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}=U_{j,k}\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}
, and
\epsilon^{(i)}_{j,k}=x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}
. Then we can write
E=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle
. Now we will show that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}=o(\sigma_{n}^{2+\delta}(a,b))
for
\delta=\frac{4}{\varepsilon}>0
. First we bound the sub-exponential norm
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
of
\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle
, which is defined in Definition
1
. One can show that

\left\|\sum_{j,k}U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}\epsilon^{(i)}_{j,k}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}\|x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}.

Then by the definition of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(C(2+\delta)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\right)^{2+\delta}\left(\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n^{-1}_{j,k}\right)^{2+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(C(2+\delta)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\right)^{2+\delta}\left(\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)^{1+\delta/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)^{1+\delta/2},

where the third line is due to the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the fact that
U_{j,k}\neq 0
only when
j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}
,
k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, or
j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
,
k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}
. In addition, for the third term in the multiplication on the third line, we have

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)^{1+\delta/2}\\
=&(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2}}\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)^{1+\delta/2}\\
\leq&(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{\delta}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-(1+\delta/2)}\\
\leq&(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\delta}(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{-\delta/2},\end{split}

(53)

where the third line is due to Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
g(x)=x^{2+\delta}
is a convex function; the fourth line is due to that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}=n_{j,k}
,
|\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}|,|\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}|\leq d_{a}+d_{b}+1
, and
n_{2}^{(a,b)}=\min\limits_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}n_{j,k}
,
n_{j,k}=n_{k,j}
. On the other hand, we can lower bound
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)
as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j,j^{\prime},k,k^{\prime}}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}U^{(\delta,i)}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}}U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,:}\Sigma^{*}(U^{(\delta,i)}_{j^{\prime},:})^{\top}+\sum_{j,k^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{j,k^{\prime}}U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,:}\Sigma^{*}U^{(\delta,i)}_{:,k^{\prime}}.

Due to the symmetry of
U^{(\delta,i)}
, we further have

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\Sigma^{*},U^{(\delta,i)}\Sigma^{*}(U^{(\delta,i)})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}U^{(\delta,i)}\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}.

Therefore, combining the upper bounds for
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}
and
\sigma_{n}^{2+\delta}(a,b)
leads to the following:

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}}{\sigma_{n}^{2+\delta}(a,b)}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{C(2+\delta)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\right)^{2+\delta}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\delta}(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{-\delta/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\epsilon}^{\delta/2}(\Sigma^{*})(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\delta}(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{-\delta/2}.

Since
\delta=\frac{4}{\epsilon}
,
C_{\epsilon}(\Sigma^{*})(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\epsilon}=o(n_{2}(a,b))
, the inequality above implies

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}=o(\sigma_{n}^{2+\delta}(a,b)).

Now we can apply the central limit theorem to obtain the following convergence in distribution result:

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{-1}(a,b)E\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

I.2.3
Normal Approximation Result

Now that we have proved an upper bound for
B
and the convergence in distribution result for
E
, a combination of these two results can lead to our final claim. To see this, note that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{-1}(a,b)\left(\theta^{(a)}_{b}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)=\frac{B}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}+\sigma_{n}^{-1}(a,b)E.

As shown earlier,

\displaystyle|B|\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}(\Theta^{*})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\lambda^{2}_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}},

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}(a,b)\geq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}{2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\frac{\min\limits_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}\left|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\right|}{\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\lambda^{2}_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\frac{\min\limits_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}\left|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\right|}{\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}.

Hence with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{B}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|\leq C(\Theta^{*};a,b)(\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}}\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}},

where

C(\Theta^{*};a,b)=\frac{C\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{3}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}\left|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\right|}

and
\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}=\frac{\lambda_{\max\limits}(\Theta^{*})}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Theta^{*})}=\frac{\lambda_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}=\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}
. When Assumptions
1
-
3
hold,
\left|\frac{B}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|=o_{p}(1)
, and hence

\sigma_{n}^{-1}(a,b)\left(\theta^{(a)}_{b}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.

As has been shown in the proof of Theorem
2
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}(a,b)=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}U^{(\delta,i)}\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}\|_{F}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\frac{\min\limits_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}\left|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\right|}{2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}.

Similarly, for the upper bound, one has

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}U^{(\delta,i)}\Sigma^{*\frac{1}{2}}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\sum_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\frac{2\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{2}^{2}}{(\Theta_{a,a}^{*})^{2}}(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{-1},

one can show that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\lambda_{\max\limits}\left(\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\otimes\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}}\right)\|U^{(\delta,i)}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\lambda^{2}_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\lambda^{2}_{\max\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{2}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{2}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}n_{2}^{(a,b)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{4}(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{-1}.

∎

I.3
Proof of Proposition
3
: Consistency of Variance Estimator

First we define the following
4
th order tensors that would be useful in our subsequent proof:
\widehat{\mathcal{T}},\mathcal{U}^{*},\widehat{\mathcal{U}},\mathcal{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p\times p\times p}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,k^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,j^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\overline{\theta}^{(a)}_{k}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j^{\prime}}\overline{\theta}^{(a)}_{k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{k}\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j^{\prime}}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}},

where
\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}=\frac{\Theta^{*}_{:,a}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
.
Also recall the definition of tensor
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}
, then we can rewrite
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)
as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\circ\mathcal{N},\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\rangle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}},\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\circ\mathcal{N}\rangle.

Define
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}-\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}^{*}
, then the estimation error for variance
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)
can be decomposed as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U}^{*}\rangle}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{T}^{*},\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\rangle}_{\mathrm{II}}+\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{2}\rangle}_{\mathrm{III}},

To establish the consistency of the variance estimator
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{n}(a,b)
, we bound the three terms above separately.

I.3.1
Bounding Error Term
\mathrm{I}

First we provide an entry-wise estimation error bound for the
4
th order moment
\mathcal{T}^{*}
.

Lemma 4

(Entry-wise error bound for
\mathcal{E}_{1}
)
.

With probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{E}_{1})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big(

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,j^{\prime}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{k,k^{\prime}}}}+\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n_{j,j^{\prime}}n_{k,k^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k^{\prime}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{k,j^{\prime}}}}+\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n_{j,k^{\prime}}n_{k,j^{\prime}}}}\Big).

Then by the definition of
\mathrm{I}
, one can show that with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\log p}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}+\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}\right)\mathcal{U}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\mathcal{U}^{*}\|_{1}\max\limits_{j,j^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},k,k^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log p}}{n_{j,k}\sqrt{n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}+\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\mathcal{U}^{*}\|_{1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}+\frac{\log p}{(n_{2}^{(a,b)})^{2}}\right)

where the second line is due to that
\mathcal{U}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}
is only nonzero for
j,j^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}
,
k,k^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
.

I.3.2
Bounding Error Term
\mathrm{II}

Since
|\mathrm{II}|\leq\|\mathcal{T}^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}\leq 2\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}
, we will show an upper bound for
\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}
in the following. By definition,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}=[\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\otimes\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\otimes\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\otimes\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\otimes\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\otimes\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\otimes\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}]\circ\mathcal{N}.

The following lemma provides a general upper bound for kronecker products.

Lemma 5
.

Consider vectors
u^{(1)},\dots,u^{(4)},\epsilon^{(1)},\dots,\epsilon^{(4)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Let
i_{l}=2\lceil l/2\rceil-{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{l\text{ is even}\}}
. If for all
1\leq l\leq 4
,

\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq\frac{C_{1}\|u^{(l)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(l)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},\quad\|\epsilon^{(l)}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\|u^{(l)}\|_{1}

for some universal constant
C_{1}
,
then we have

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(i_{l})}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(l)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq l,i_{l}}\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}\right],

where
C_{2}
is also a universal constant.

We can apply Lemma
5
with
u^{(1)}=u^{(3)}=\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}
,
u^{(2)}=u^{(4)}=\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}
,
\epsilon^{(1)}=\epsilon^{(3)}=\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}
,
\epsilon^{(2)}=\epsilon^{(4)}=\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}=-\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}+\theta^{(a)*}
. By Lemma
3
,

\displaystyle\|\epsilon^{(1)}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{b}^{(a)}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}d^{(a)}_{b}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{k\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}=C\|u^{(1)}\|_{1},

where we have applied the sample size condition in Assumption
1
. In addition, Lemma
3
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\frac{d_{b}^{(a)}\sqrt{\log p}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{k}^{(a)}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}.

Furthermore, Theorem
7
suggests that

\displaystyle\|\epsilon^{(2)}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}d_{a}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{|\epsilon^{(2)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\frac{d_{a}\sqrt{\log p}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{i\in[p]}n_{i,k}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}},

which further implies that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{II}|\leq\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}.

I.3.3
Bounding Error Term
\mathrm{III}

By Lemma
4
, one can bound
\mathrm{III}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{(\log p)n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}+\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}\right)|(\mathcal{E}_{2})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\sqrt{\log p}\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{\sqrt{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|(\mathcal{E}_{2})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle+C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\log p\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{|(\mathcal{E}_{2})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\sqrt{\log p}\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}+C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\log p\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1},

where
\mathcal{N}^{(1)},\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p\times p\times p}
satisfy
\mathcal{N}^{(1)}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{\sqrt{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
and
\mathcal{N}^{(2)}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
. By Lemma
13
(a similar result to Lemma
5
but with
\mathcal{N}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{N}^{(2)}
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{2}},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{5/2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{3}_{\infty}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{2}}.

Combining the upper bounds for
\mathrm{I}
,
\mathrm{II}
, and
\mathrm{III}
, and applying Assumptions
1
and
3
, one can show that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}.

To interpret the bound above, note that
\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}
can be viewed as an upper bound for
|\langle\mathcal{T}^{*},\mathcal{U}^{*}\rangle|\leq\|\mathcal{T}^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\mathcal{U}^{*}\|_{1}
,
\frac{\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}
is the error brought by estimating
\mathcal{U}^{*}=\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\otimes\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\otimes\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\otimes\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}
, which dominates the error brought by estimating
\mathcal{T}^{*}
. As have been shown in the proof of Theorem
2
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)\geq\frac{\lambda_{\min\limits}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\min\limits_{(j,k)\in S_{2}(a,b)}\left|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\right|^{2}}{2(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}n_{2}^{(a,b)}},

which implies

\begin{split}&\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)|}{\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)}\\
\leq&\frac{C^{2}(\Theta^{*};a,b)}{\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)n_{2}^{(a,b)}\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}\\
=:&\varepsilon_{n}.\end{split}

(54)

By Assumption
4
,
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0
. Note that when
\varepsilon_{n}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
, one has

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}(a,b)|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)}{2\sqrt{\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)-|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{n}\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}},

and hence,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1\right|=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)-\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}(a,b)|}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)-|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}(a,b)|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}-\varepsilon_{n}}.

Since
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}-\varepsilon_{n}}=0
, for any
\delta>0
, there exists
n_{0}
such that if
n>n_{0}
,
\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}-\varepsilon_{n}}\leq\delta
, and thus

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1\right|>\delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)|}{\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)}>\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}-\varepsilon_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}Cp_{n}^{-c}=0.

Here we write
p=p(n)
to reflect the fact that
p
also tends to
\infty
as
n
tends to
\infty
. Therefore,
\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}1
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Combine the results in Theorem
2
and Proposition
3
, and apply Slutsky’s theorem, the proof is then complete.
∎

I.4
Proof of Theorem
4

Under the null hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{0}:\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=0
, Theorem
3
suggests that
\widehat{z}(a,b)\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and hence
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq z_{\alpha/2})=2F_{Z}(-z_{\alpha/2})=\alpha
.

While under the alternative hypothesis
\mathcal{H}_{A}:\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}=\delta_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{z}(a,b)=\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}+\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b}).

If
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}=\delta
for some
\delta\in\mathbb{R}
, then Proposition
3
suggests that
\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\delta
. By Slutsky’s theorem and Theorem
3
,
\widehat{z}(a,b)\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(\delta,1)
, and hence

\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq z_{\alpha/2})=F_{Z}(-z_{\alpha/2}+\delta)+F_{Z}(-z_{\alpha/2}-\delta)\geq F_{Z}(|\delta|-z_{\alpha/2}).

In particular, when
\delta=0
,
F_{Z}(-z_{\alpha/2}+\delta)+F_{Z}(-z_{\alpha/2}-\delta)=\alpha
, and
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq z_{\alpha/2})=\alpha.

Now we consider the last case:
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\frac{|\delta_{n}|}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}=+\infty
. Since

|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq\left|\frac{\delta_{n}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\right|-\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})\right|,

we have that for any
\delta>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|<z_{\alpha/2})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>\left|\frac{\delta_{n}}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\right|-z_{\alpha/2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>\left|\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|(1-\delta)-z_{\alpha/2})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}(|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1|>\delta).

Recall the error term
\varepsilon_{n}
defined in (
54
). Let
\delta=\frac{\varepsilon_{n}}{2\sqrt{1-\varepsilon_{n}}-\varepsilon_{n}}
, and recall that in the proof of Proposition
3
, we have shown that
\mathbb{P}(|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1|>\delta)\leq Cp^{-c}
. Since
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\delta=0
,
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\frac{|\delta_{n}|}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}=+\infty
, one has

C_{n}:=\lim_{n,p\rightarrow}\left|\frac{\delta_{n}}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|(1-\delta)-z_{\alpha/2}=+\infty.

Hence for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
N_{1}
such that if
n>N_{1}
,
C_{n}>z_{\epsilon/4}
; there also exists
N_{2}
such that if
n>N_{2}
,
|\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>z_{\epsilon/4})-\mathbb{P}(|Z|>z_{\epsilon/4})|<\epsilon/2
, where
Z
follows standard Gaussian distribution. Therefore, for
n\geq N_{1}\vee N_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>C_{n})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>z_{\epsilon/4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|Z|>z_{\epsilon/4})+\epsilon/2

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon,

which implies that
\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})|>C_{n})=0
and hence

\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq z_{\alpha/2})=1.

I.5
Proof of Theorem
9
: Valid Inference for the Precision Matrix

Now we show the coverage guarantee of our confidence interval
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}^{a,b}_{\Theta,\alpha}
defined in (
23
) and (
24
). We will focus on the
a\neq b
case first, and then present the proof for the
a=b
case, which can be built on top of the previous proof steps. In particular, for the
a\neq b
case, we first prove the normal approximation result for
\frac{\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}
, and then we show the variance estimation consistency of
\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)
.

I.5.1
Decomposition for
\frac{\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}

By the definition of
\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}
, one can show that

\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})-(\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a})(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})-(\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a})\theta^{(a)*}_{b}.

Theorem
2
suggests that

-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})=-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}B-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}E,

where
|B|\leq C(\Theta^{*},\gamma_{a},\gamma^{(a)}_{b})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}
with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
, and
\sigma_{n}^{-1}(a,b)E\overset{\text{d}}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Now we focus on the latter two terms and aim to decompose them into terms that are higher order biases and error terms that converges to a normal distribution. We start with a decomposition of
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}
as a function of
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}^{-1}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}
. By the definition of
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}
, we have that

\begin{split}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}^{-1}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}=&\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*\top}\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\\
=&\underbrace{\overline{\theta}^{(a)*\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}}_{\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}}+\underbrace{\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}}_{\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}}.\end{split}

(55)

With a little abuse of notations, we decompose
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}^{-1}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}
into two error terms
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}
and
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}
. We also let
\varepsilon^{(1)}=\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}+\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}
, while the three notations
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}
,
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}
, and
\varepsilon^{(1)}
might be defined differently in the proofs of other theoretical statements. Then we can write

\begin{split}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}=&-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}(\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}^{-1}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1})\\
=&-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}[\Theta^{*}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\varepsilon^{(1)}]\varepsilon^{(1)}\\
=&-\underbrace{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}}_{\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}}-\underbrace{[(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}-\varepsilon^{(1)2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}]}_{\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}},\end{split}

(56)

with
\varepsilon^{(2)}=\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}
.
With these decompositions in place, we have

\begin{split}\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=\underbrace{\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}\theta^{(a)*}_{b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}E}_{E^{(2)}}+\underbrace{\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}\theta^{(a)*}_{b}+\varepsilon^{(2)}(\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}-\theta^{(a)*}_{b})-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}B}_{B^{(2)}},\end{split}

(57)

and in the following we will show an upper bound for
B^{(2)}
based off bounds for
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}
,
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}
, and central limit theorem for

\begin{split}E^{(2)}=&\left\langle\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*},-\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*},-\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\right\rangle.\end{split}

I.5.2
Bounding
B^{(2)}

For the first error term
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}
in
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}^{-1}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}
, one can show that

\begin{split}|\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}|\leq&\|\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}^{2}\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}-\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\|_{\infty}\\
\leq&C\frac{\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}},\end{split}

where we have applied the entry-wise error bound for
\widehat{\Sigma}
in Lemma
2
in the second line, and
n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}
was defined as
n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}=\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}(a,b)\cup\{(a,a)\}}n_{j,k}
. While for
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}
, we show in the following that this is a higher-order error:

\begin{split}|\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}|\leq&2|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}|+|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})|\\
\leq&2|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}|+2|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}|\\
&+|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}\Sigma^{*}(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})|+|(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})|\\
\leq&\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})\|_{\infty}(2\|\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1})+\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

where in the last line, we have applied the fact that
(\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})_{j}=0
for all
j\neq a
and
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{a}=\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{a}=1
, which implies
(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})^{\top}\Sigma^{*}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}=0
. Furthermore, our covariance error shown in Lemma
2
suggests that

\begin{split}\|(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})(\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*})\|_{\infty}\leq&C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\max_{j}\sum_{k=1}^{p}|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{k}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{k}|\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{\lambda^{(a)},1}}{\|\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}},\end{split}

where we have applied Theorem
7
in the last line. Furthermore, applying the
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
norm bounds in Theorem
7
leads to the following:

|\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}|\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\frac{d_{a}\log p}{\min_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}}.

(58)

While for
\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}
, we have

\begin{split}|\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}|\leq&\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}-\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\|_{\infty}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{j,k\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}},\end{split}

(59)

where
S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)
in the last line was defined as
S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)=S_{1}(a,b)\cup\{(a,a)\}
in Assumption
8
, and we have applied Lemma
2
. Combining (
59
) and (
58
) together, and recalling Assumption
8
, we have
|\varepsilon^{(1)}|\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{j,k\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}
. Note that Assumption
8
implies
|\varepsilon^{(1)}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{j,k\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\leq\frac{1}{2}(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}
, which further suggests that
|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}|\leq\frac{1}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{-1}-|\varepsilon^{(1)}|}\leq 2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}
. Therefore, by the definition of
\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}
and
\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}
in (
56
), one can show that
|\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{j,k\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}
, while for
\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}
, some more calculations show that

\begin{split}|\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}|\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+1)\log p}{\min_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}}\\
&+\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{4}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\frac{\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+1)\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}},\end{split}

where the last line is due to the fact that
\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\leq(d_{a}+1)\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{2}^{4}\leq(d_{a}+1)^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{-2}(\Sigma^{*})
and
\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\geq\lambda_{\max}^{-1}(\Sigma^{*})
. Combining bounds for
\varepsilon^{(2)}_{1}
and
\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}
and applying Assumption
8
, we have

|\varepsilon^{(2)}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}\leq C\Theta^{*}_{a,a}.

Now we plug in these bounds into (
57
) and apply Theorem
2
to bound
B^{(2)}
as follows:

\begin{split}|B^{(2)}|\leq&|\varepsilon^{(2)}_{2}||\theta^{(a)*}_{b}|+|\varepsilon^{(2)}||E|+C\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|B|\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+1)\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}\\
&+C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{\min_{(j,k)\in S_{1}^{\prime}(a,b)}n_{j,k}}}|E|\\
&+C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}},\end{split}

where
B
is bounded in Theorem
2
; While for
E
, the specific form was defined in the proof of Theorem
2
:
E=\frac{\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}(\widehat{\Sigma}-\Sigma^{*})\Theta^{*}_{:,a}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
, and we bound it by applying Lemma
2
: with probability at least
1-p^{-c}
,

\begin{split}|E|\leq&\frac{\left\|\widehat{\Sigma}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}-\Sigma^{*}_{\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b},\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\right\|_{1}\left\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\right\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}.\end{split}

Therefore, we can further simplify the bound for
|B^{(2)}|
as

|B^{(2)}|\leq C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}(\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|)\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}},

with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
.

I.5.3
Central limit theorem for
E^{(2)}

To show the normal approximation for
E^{(2)}
, the proof is mostly the same as that of Theorem
2
. First note that we can also write
E^{(2)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}[(x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{*})\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N]\Theta^{*}_{:,b}
, sum of independent random variables. The variance
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)
can be similarly computed as

\begin{split}\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\Theta^{*}_{a,:}[(x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}-\Sigma^{*})\circ\delta^{(i)}\oslash N]\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\right)^{2}\\
=&\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{Var}\left(\sum_{j,k}\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{k,b}(x_{i,j}x_{i,k}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{a,j^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{k^{\prime},b}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}\mathcal{T}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}\delta^{(i)}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\\
=&\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\times_{3}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{:,b}.\end{split}

Similar to the proof of Theorem
2
, now we verify the Lyapounov’s condition. We define
U,U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
by
U_{j,k}=\frac{1}{2}(\Theta^{*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a})
,

U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}=U_{j,k}\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}
, and
\epsilon^{(i)}_{j,k}=x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}
. Then we can write
E^{(2)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle
. Note that the definition of
U,U^{(\delta,i)}
are different from those in the proof of Theorem
2
. Let
\delta=\frac{4}{\epsilon}>0
for the constant
\epsilon>0
in Assumption
9
. Following the same arguments as in Section
I.2.2
, one can show that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}\leq\left(C(2+\delta)\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\right)^{2+\delta}\left(\sum_{j,k}|U_{j,k}|^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}\right)^{1+\delta/2}(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})^{-\delta/2}.

Furthermore, it can be shown that

\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)\geq 2\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\min_{(j,k)\in S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)}|\Theta^{*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}|^{2}(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})^{-1}.

(60)

Therefore, we have

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2+\delta}(a,b)}\leq C_{\epsilon}^{\delta/2}(\Sigma^{*})(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2+\delta}(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})^{-\delta/2},

and hence Lyapounov’s condition holds:

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}|\langle U^{(\delta,i)},\epsilon^{(i)}\rangle|^{2+\delta}=o(\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2+\delta}(a,b))
. Now we can apply the central limit theorem to obtain the following convergence in distribution result:

\displaystyle\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{-1}(a,b)E^{(2)}\overset{d}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Recall the definition
S_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}=(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b})\cup(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a})
in Assumption
9
.

Recall the decomposition
57
, we have

\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b})=\frac{B^{(2)}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}+\frac{E^{(2)}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)},

where the second term converges to
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and the first term is bounded as follows with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
:

\left|\frac{B^{(2)}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}\right|\leq C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)(\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}},

where
C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)=\frac{C\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}})]}{\lambda_{\min}(\Theta^{*})\min_{(j,k)\in S_{2}^{\prime}(a,b)}|\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{b,k}+\Theta^{*}_{a,k}\Theta^{*}_{b,j}|}
, and we have applied the fact that
\|\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\|_{1}=\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}-\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\leq\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
.
Assumption
10
implies that
\frac{B^{(2)}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}=o_{p}(1)
and hence
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{-1}(a,b)(\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}-\Theta^{*}_{a,b})\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

I.5.4
Consistency of variance estimate

Now it remains to show that
\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}1
. Recall that we have already proved the consistency of variance estimate
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}\times_{1}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{2}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}\times_{3}\widehat{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}\times_{4}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
for
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\times_{2}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\times_{3}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\times_{4}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}
, the variance associated with
\widetilde{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}
. While in this proof, the true variance parameter associated with
\widetilde{\Theta}_{a,b}
is
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\times_{3}\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
, and its estimate is
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}\times_{1}\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}\times_{2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}\times_{3}\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}\times_{4}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}
. The only difference lies in the parameters multiplied with the covariance matrix
\mathcal{T}^{(n)}
or its estimate
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}
. Hence we can follow the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3
to show the consistency of
\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)
for
\sigma^{2}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)
, and we only highlight the key steps that differ from that proof.

Define
\mathcal{U}^{*},\widehat{\mathcal{U}},\mathcal{N}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p\times p\times p}
as
4
th order tensors that satisfy

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,k}\Theta^{*}_{b,j^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}_{b,j}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,k}\widehat{\Theta}_{b,j^{\prime}}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,k^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}.

Recall that we proposed several options for
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}
. For simplicity, here we focus on the setting where
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}=\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
, and
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}=\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}
, while noting that all proof steps still hold if other options of
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}
are considered.

Also recall the definition of tensor
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(n)}
, then we can rewrite
\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)
as follows

\displaystyle\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\circ\mathcal{N},\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\rangle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}},\widehat{\mathcal{U}}\circ\mathcal{N}\rangle,

as an estimate of

\displaystyle\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=\langle\mathcal{T}^{*}\circ\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U}^{*}\rangle=\langle\mathcal{T}^{*},\mathcal{U}^{*}\circ\mathcal{N}\rangle.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}-\mathcal{T}^{*}
,
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\widehat{\mathcal{U}}-\mathcal{U}^{*}
. Then the estimation error for variance can be decomposed as follows:

\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)=\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U}^{*}\rangle}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{T}^{*},\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\rangle}_{\mathrm{II}}+\underbrace{\langle\mathcal{E}_{1}\circ\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E}_{2}\rangle}_{\mathrm{III}},

(61)

This is the same as the decomposition of
\widehat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)
but with different definitions of
\mathcal{U}^{*}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}
. Following the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3
,
|\mathrm{I}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\mathcal{U}^{*}\|_{1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})^{3/2}}+\frac{\log p}{(n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)})^{2}}\right)
, where
\|\mathcal{U}^{*}\|_{1}=\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}^{2}\|\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\|_{1}^{2}
.

While for error terms
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{III}
, following the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3
, the problem still reduces to bounding
\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}
,
\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}
, and
\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}
, where
\mathcal{N}^{(1)},\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p\times p\times p}
satisfy
\mathcal{N}^{(1)}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{\sqrt{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
and
\mathcal{N}^{(2)}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}
. To achieve this, here we first present general versions of
5
and Lemma
13
.

Lemma 6
.

Consider vectors
u^{(1)},\dots,u^{(4)},\epsilon^{(1)},\dots,\epsilon^{(4)}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Let
i_{l}=l+1-2{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{l\text{ is even}\}}
,
j_{l}=l+2-4{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{l>2\}}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\left(\frac{\|u^{(i_{l})}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(l)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(i_{l})}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right)\prod_{m\neq l,i_{l}}\left(\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}+\|\epsilon^{(m)}\|_{1}\right)\right],

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{l=1}^{4}\Bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\left(\frac{\|u^{(i_{l})}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(i_{l})})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(i_{l})}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\|u^{(j_{l})}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(j_{l})})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(j_{l})}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right)\prod_{m\neq l,i_{l},j_{l}}(\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}+\|\epsilon^{(m)}\|_{1})\Bigg],

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq l}\left(\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(m)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right)\right],

where
C
is a universal constant.

We would like to apply Lemma
6
with
u^{(1)}=u^{(3)}=\Theta^{*}_{b,:}
,
u^{(2)}=u^{(4)}=\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
,
\epsilon^{(1)}=\epsilon^{(3)}=\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}-\Theta^{*}_{b,:}
, and
\epsilon^{(2)}=\epsilon^{(4)}=\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}-\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
. Now what remain to show are the
\ell_{1}
and weighted
\ell_{1}
bounds for
\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}-\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
and
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}-\Theta^{*}_{b,:}
. First note that

\begin{split}|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,j}-\Theta^{*}_{a,j}|\leq|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}||\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|+|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{j}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}||\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}||\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{j}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|,\end{split}

and hence

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}-\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}\leq&|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\|\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}|\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1},\\
\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\Theta}_{a,j}-\Theta^{*}_{a,j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq&|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right||\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\\
&+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|,\end{split}

where we have applied the fact that
\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}=-(\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*})
.
Recall from Theorem
7
that

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{a}^{2}\log p}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},\\
\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\frac{d_{a}\sqrt{\log p}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{a}}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}.\end{split}

Also, we just showed the bounds for
\varepsilon^{(2)}=|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|
in Section
I.5.2
:

|\varepsilon^{(2)}|\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\leq C\Theta^{*}_{a,a}.

(62)

Therefore,

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,:}-\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+1)(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\leq C\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1},\\
\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\Theta}_{a,j}-\Theta^{*}_{a,j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\leq\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}.\end{split}

where we have applied Assumption
8
in the second inequalities.

While for
\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}-\Theta^{*}_{b,:}
, note that
\Theta^{*}_{b,:}=\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}+\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,b}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}
, and hence

\begin{split}|\widehat{\Theta}_{b,j}-\Theta^{*}_{b,j}|\leq&|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{j}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|\\
\leq&|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}||\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|+|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}||\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{j}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|\\
&+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}||\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}_{j}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}|,\end{split}

which implies that

\begin{split}\|\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}-\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\leq&\|\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\|_{1}+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|\|\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}\\
&+|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|\|\widehat{\overline{\theta}}^{(a)}-\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}\|_{1}\\
\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\Theta}_{b,j}-\Theta^{*}_{b,j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq&\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,j}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}||\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\overline{\theta}^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right||\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{j}-\theta^{(a)*}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|.\end{split}

The associated upper bounds for
\widetilde{\Theta}^{(a)}_{b,:}-\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}
and
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}
are established in Lemma
3
and Theorem
7
. While for the error term
|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|
, we bound it as follows:

\begin{split}|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|\leq&|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}||\theta^{(a))*}_{b}|+\Theta^{*}_{a,a}|\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\theta^{(a)*}_{b}|+|\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}||\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}_{b}+\theta^{(a)*}_{b}|\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{2}\right)(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}(|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|+\Theta^{*}_{a,a})(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}},\end{split}

where we have applied (
62
) and the
\ell_{2}
-norm err bound for
\widehat{\theta}^{(a)}-\theta^{(a)*}
in Theorem
7
in the second line, and the third line is due to the fact that
\frac{\|\Theta^{*}_{:.a}\|_{2}^{2}}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{\max}(\Theta^{*})}{\lambda_{\min}(\Theta^{*})}=\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}
.
Combining the bounds above with Lemma
3
and Theorem
7
, we have

\begin{split}&\|\widehat{\Theta}_{b,:}-\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\|_{1}\\
\leq&\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right](d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\\
\leq&C\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right],\\
&\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\widehat{\Theta}_{b,j}-\Theta^{*}_{b,j}}{\sqrt{\min_{k}n_{j,k}}}\right|\\
\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}}{\lambda_{\min\limits}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}\\
\leq&\frac{C\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}},\end{split}

where we have applied Assumption
8
in the last inequalities.
Now we apply Lemma
6
and obtain the following bounds for
\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}
,
\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}
,
\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}
:

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{3/2}},\\
\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{2}},\\
\|\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{5/2}}.\end{split}

Therefore, using the same steps as in the proof of Proposition
3
, we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{II}\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{3/2}},\\
\mathrm{III}\leq&\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{2}}.\end{split}

Finally, plugging in the bounds for
\mathrm{I}
,
\mathrm{II}
, and
\mathrm{III}
into (
61
), one directly has

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\left[\|\Theta^{*}_{:,b}\|_{1}+\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}\left(1+\frac{|\Theta^{*}_{a,b}|}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}\right)\right]^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)})^{3/2}}.

Recall the lower bound for
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)
we have shown earlier in (
60
), which implies the following:

\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)-\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)|}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,b)}\leq C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,b)^{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}}\frac{n_{2}^{\prime(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,b)}}.

Following the same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3
, and applying Assumption
11
, we arrive at our conclusion that
\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,b)}-1\overset{p}{\rightarrow}0
. Therefore, the proof is now complete.

I.5.5
Proof for the
a=b
case

Now we show that for the
a=b
case, the confidence interval
\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{\Theta,\alpha}^{a,a}
is also asymptotically valid under Assumptions
8
-
11
. Let
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)=\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{3}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
. We will show that
\frac{\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,a)}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and
\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)
is a consistent estimator for
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)
.

Recall that we have shown the following decomposition in Section
I.5.1
:

\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,a}=-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{1}-[(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}-\varepsilon^{(1)2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}].

Following the same steps as in Section
I.5.2
and Section
I.5.3
, one can show that with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\left|(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}-\varepsilon^{(1)2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\right|\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\Sigma^{*})\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\frac{(d_{a}+1)\log p}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,a)}},

and
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)\geq\frac{2\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}(\Theta^{*}_{j,a})^{4}}{n_{2}^{\prime(a,a)}}
. Therefore, Assumption
10
suggests that

\frac{\left|(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon^{(1)}_{2}-\varepsilon^{(1)2}\widehat{\Theta}_{a,a}(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\right|}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,a)}\leq C^{\prime}(\Theta^{*};a,a)\kappa_{\Sigma^{*}}(da+1)\log p\frac{\sqrt{n_{2}^{\prime(a,a)}}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,a)}}=o(1).

Furthermore, following the same argument as in Section
I.5.3
, one can show that
\frac{-(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}\varepsilon_{1}^{(1)}}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}(a,a)}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}(0,1)
based on Assumption
9
. Therefore, the final step is to show that
\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,a)}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}1
. Still, we follow the same arguments as in Section
I.5.3
, and achieve the following bound:

|\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)-\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)|\leq C\kappa^{2}_{\Sigma^{*}}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\|\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\|_{1}^{4}\frac{(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{\prime(a,a)})^{{3/2}}}.

Putting this error bound above and the lower bound
\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)\geq\frac{2\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\min_{j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}}(\Theta^{*}_{j,a})^{4}}{n_{2}^{\prime(a,a)}}
together, one has

\frac{|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}_{n,\Theta}(a,a)-\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)|}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)}\leq C^{\prime 2}(\Theta;a,a)\frac{(d_{a}+1)\sqrt{\log p}}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{\prime(a,a)}}}\frac{n_{2}^{\prime(a,a)}}{n_{1}^{\prime(a,a)}},

with high probability. A direct application of Assumption
11
verifies the final step:
\frac{\widehat{\sigma}_{n,\Theta}(a,a)}{\sigma_{n,\Theta}^{2}(a,a)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}1
. The proof is now complete.

I.6
Proof of Theorem
5

Here, we provide the detailed proofs of Theorem
5
with Assumptions
5
and
6
. Let
m=p(p-1)/2
be the number of edge-wise tests,
m_{0}=\{(j,k)\in[p]:\Theta^{*}_{j,k}=0\}
be the number of node pairs for which the null hypothesis holds true, and the Gaussian tail probability function
G(t)=2(1-\Phi(t))
for
t\geq 0
. Recall that
t_{p}=\sqrt{2\log m-2\log\log m}
,

\mathrm{FDP}=\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{p_{(a,b)}\leq\rho_{0}\}}}{R(\rho_{0})\vee 1}=\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t_{0}\}}}{R(\rho_{0})\vee 1},

where
t_{0}=G^{-1}(\rho_{0})
,
\rho_{0}=\sup\left\{G(t_{p})\leq\rho\leq 1:\frac{m\rho}{R(\rho)\vee 1}\leq\alpha\right\}
, if there exists
G(t_{p})\leq\rho\leq 1
such that
\frac{m\rho}{R(\rho)\vee 1}\leq\alpha
;
otherwise,
\rho_{0}=G(\sqrt{2\log m})
.

Case I:
\rho_{0}=G(\sqrt{2\log m})

We first show that when
\rho_{0}=G(\sqrt{2\log m})
and
t_{0}=\sqrt{2\log m}
, FDP
\overset{p}{\rightarrow}0
. Applying the decomposition in Theorem
2
to edge
(a,b)
, we have
\widehat{z}(a,b)=\frac{B(a,b)+E(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}
if
(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, where

\frac{B(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\leq C(\Theta^{*};a,b)(\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}+\sqrt{\gamma_{a}}+\sqrt{\gamma^{(a)}_{b}})\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p}{\sqrt{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}}\sqrt{\frac{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}}:=\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}

with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
, and the proof of Proposition
3
implies that with the same probability,

\left|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1\right|\leq\frac{C^{2}(\Theta^{*};a,b)}{\kappa_{\Theta^{*}}^{2}}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)n_{2}^{(a,b)}\sqrt{\log p}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}:=\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}.

Hence some calculations show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{FDP}>0)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq\sqrt{2\log m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{B(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}+\frac{E(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\log m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{B(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|>\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}-1\right|>\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|\geq\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cp^{-c}+\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|\geq\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}\right).

The following lemma concentrates the tail bound of
\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}
around the Gaussian tail.

Lemma 7
.

For any node pair
(a,b)
,
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon>0

\displaystyle\max\{\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|>t\right)-G(t-\varepsilon),G(t+\varepsilon)-\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|>t\right)\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\exp\left\{-\frac{c\varepsilon\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}}\right\}.

Applying Lemma
7
with
\varepsilon=(\log p)^{-2}
gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{FDP}>0)\leq

\displaystyle Cp^{-c}+\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}G\left(\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}-(\log p)^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle+Cp^{2}\exp\left\{-\frac{c\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}(\log p)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cp^{-c}+\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}G\left(\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}-(\log p)^{-2}\right),

where the last line is due to Assumption
5
. While for the last term, let
x=t_{0}-(\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}-(\log p)^{-2})
, and we will also need the following lemma on Gaussian tail bound:

Lemma 8
.

For any
t>0
,

\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}(t+1/t)}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}\leq G(t)\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}t}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2}}.

Lemma
8
is a standard Gaussian tail bound, which also appears in
[
27
]
.
Hence we have

\displaystyle G(t_{0}-x)\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}(t_{0}-x)}e^{-\frac{(t_{0}-x)^{2}}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{2\pi}(t_{0}-x)}e^{-\frac{t_{0}^{2}-2t_{0}x}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}(t_{0}-x)p^{2}}e^{\sqrt{\log p}x}.

Further note that

\displaystyle x=

\displaystyle t_{0}-\left(\frac{t_{0}}{1+\varepsilon^{(2)}_{a,b}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b}-(\log p)^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\log m}\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)}+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)n_{2}^{(a,b)}\log p}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}+C_{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\log p\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}+(\log p)^{-2},

and applying Assumption
5
leads to

\sqrt{\log p}x\leq\frac{C}{\log p}
, which implies

G(t_{0}-x)\leq\frac{C}{p^{2}\sqrt{\log p}}.

Therefore, when
t_{0}=\sqrt{2\log m}
,

\mathbb{E}(\mathrm{FDP})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{FDP}>0)\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{\log p}},

where we have applied the boundedness of
\mathrm{FDP}
in the inequality above.

Case II:
\rho_{0}=\sup\left\{G(t_{p})\leq\rho\leq 1:\frac{m\rho}{R(\rho)\vee 1}\leq\alpha\right\}

Now suppose that

\rho_{0}=\sup\left\{G(t_{p})\leq\rho\leq 1:\frac{m\rho}{R(\rho)\vee 1}\leq\alpha\right\},

and
t_{0}=G^{-1}(\rho_{0})
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{FDP}=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t_{0}\}}}{R(\rho_{0})\vee 1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}({{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t_{0}\}}-\rho_{0})\right|+\rho_{0}m}{R(\rho_{0})\vee 1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha\left(1+\frac{\left|\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}({{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t_{0}\}}-G(t_{0}))\right|}{mG(t_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha\left(1+\sup_{0\leq t\leq t_{p}}\frac{\left|\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}({{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t\}}-G(t))\right|}{mG(t)}\right).

In fact, it suffices to show that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq t_{p}}\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t\}}}{m_{0}G(t)}-1\right|\rightarrow 0

(63)

in probability, which immediately implies that

\lim_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{FDP}>\alpha+\epsilon)=0
; In addition, by the boundedness of the quantity above, convergence in probability also implies convergence in expectation and hence we would have
\limsup_{n,p\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{FDR}\leq\alpha
.

We now discretize the interval
[0,t_{p}]
into
0=x_{1}<x_{2}<\dots<x_{K}=t_{p}
, where
x_{k}-x_{k-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}}
. In the following, we show that (
63
) can be implied by

\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{k})}-1\right|\rightarrow 0

(64)

in probability.
Suppose for now that (
64
) holds, then for any
t\in[0,t_{p}]
, there exists
1\leq l\leq K

such that
x_{l}\leq t\leq x_{l+1}
, and hence

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{l+1}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{l})}-1\leq\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t\}}}{m_{0}G(t)}-1\leq\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{l}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{l+1})}-1

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq t\}}}{m_{0}G(t)}-1\right|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{k})}-1\right|\frac{G(x_{l})}{G(x_{l+1})}+\left|\frac{G(x_{l})}{G(x_{l+1})}-1\right|.

Let
\phi(\cdot)
be the density function of standard Gaussian distribution. Now note that

\frac{G(x_{l+1})}{G(x_{l})}\geq 1-\frac{2(x_{l+1}-x_{l})\phi(x_{l})}{G(x_{l})}
, and Lemma
8
suggests

\frac{\phi(x_{l})}{G(x_{l})}\leq\begin{cases}\frac{x_{l}+1}{2}\leq t_{p},&x_{l}\geq 1,\\
\frac{\phi(0)}{G(1)},&0\leq x_{l}\leq 1.\end{cases}

Hence
|\frac{G(x_{l+1})}{G(x_{l})}-1|\leq c\sqrt{\log\log m}\rightarrow 0
, and (
64
) implies (
63
).

The rest of the proof is devoted to showing (
63
). For any
\epsilon>0
, some calculations show that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq K}\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{k})}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)\\
\leq&\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{k})}-1\right|>\epsilon\right)\\
\leq&\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left(\left|\frac{\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}{{\mathbbm{1}}}_{\{|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k}\}}}{m_{0}G(x_{k})}-1\right|^{2}\right)\\
=&\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1}{m_{0}^{2}}\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0},(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k},|\widehat{z}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})|\geq x_{k})}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)\\
&-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{2}{m_{0}}\sum_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k})}{G(x_{k})}-1\right),\end{split}

(65)

and we will lower bound
\mathrm{I}(a,b)=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k})}{G(x_{k})}-1\right)
, and upper bound
\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k},|\widehat{z}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})|\geq x_{k})}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)
, respectively.

Bounding
\mathrm{I}(a,b)
:

We first follow similar arguments to case I. Recall the decompositions and error bounds in the proof of Theorem
2
and Proposition
3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)|\geq x_{k})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{B(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}+\frac{E(a,b)}{\widehat{\sigma}_{n}(a,b)}\right|\geq x_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|\geq x_{k}(1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)})+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}\right)-Cp^{-c}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle G(x_{k}(1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)})+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2})-Cp^{-c}

\displaystyle-C\exp\left\{-\frac{c\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}(\log p)^{2}}\right\},

where we applied Lemma
7
with
\varepsilon=(\log p)^{-2}
on the last line. Since
G(x_{k})\geq G(t_{p})\geq\frac{c}{t_{p}}e^{-\frac{t_{p}^{2}}{2}}\geq\frac{c}{p^{2}\sqrt{\log p}}
,
K=t_{p}\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}+1\leq C\log p\sqrt{\log\log p}
,
one can show that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(a,b)\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{G(x_{k}(1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)})+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2})-G(x_{k})}{G(x_{k})}

\displaystyle-C\exp\left\{3\log p-\frac{c\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}(\log p)^{2}}\right\}-Cp^{-c}.

Assumption
5
