implies that the last two terms both converge to zero, thus it suffices to show that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{G(x_{k}(1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)})+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2})-G(x_{k})}{G(x_{k})}\rightarrow 0
. In fact,

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{G(x_{k})-G(x_{k}(1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)})+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2})}{G(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{(x_{k}\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)}+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2}))\phi(x_{k})}{G(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ct_{p}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(t_{p}\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)}+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)}+(\log p)^{-2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)(\log p)^{\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\log\log p}n_{2}^{(a,b)}}{(n_{1}^{(a,b)})^{3/2}}+C_{2}\frac{(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)(\log p)^{\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\log\log p}\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}{n_{1}^{(a,b)}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log\log p}{\log p}},

and Assumption
5
suggests the right hand side of the inequality above would converge to zero.

Bounding
\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
:

For any node pairs
(a,b)
and
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, we can utilize the same decomposition for the test statistics
\widehat{z}(a,b)
and
\widehat{z}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
. Following similar arguments to bounding
\mathrm{I}(a,b)
, one can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widehat{z}(a,b)\geq x_{k},\widehat{z}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\geq x_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cp^{-c}+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma(a,b)}\right|\geq\frac{x_{k}}{1+\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b},\left|\frac{E(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}{\sigma(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}\right|\geq\frac{x_{k}}{1+\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(2)}}-\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\right).

The following lemma suggests that the tail bound for
(\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma(a,b)},\frac{E(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}{\sigma(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})})
can be well approximated by the tail bound for a bivariate normal distribution with the same covariance matrix.

Lemma 9
.

For any node pairs
(a,b)
and
(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
,
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
,
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\right|>t_{1},\left|\frac{E(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}{\sigma_{n}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}\right|>t_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|z_{1}\right|>t_{1}-\varepsilon,\left|z_{2}\right|>t_{2}-\varepsilon\right)+C\exp\left\{-\frac{c\varepsilon\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}\vee n_{2}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d+1)\alpha(\Theta^{*},\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})}\right\},

where
(z_{1},z_{2})
follows bivariate normal distribution with the same mean and covariance matrix as
(\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)},\frac{E(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}{\sigma_{n}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})})
.

We can apply Lemma
9
with
\varepsilon=(\log p)^{-2}
, and obtain the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{z}(a,b)\geq x_{k},|\widehat{z}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})|\geq x_{k})}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cp^{-c}+C\exp\left\{C\log p-\frac{c\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}\vee n_{2}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}}{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d+1)\alpha(\Theta^{*},\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})}(\log p)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right),

where
x_{k}^{\prime}=\frac{x_{k}}{1+\max\{\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)},\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(2)}\}}-\max\{\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a,b},\varepsilon^{(1)}_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}\}-(\log p)^{-2}
.
Assumption
5
implies that the first two terms both converge to zero, hence we will focus on the last term in the following analysis. We will discuss three different cases separately: (i)
(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})

\in\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})
; (ii)
(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})

\in\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)
; (iii)
(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})

\in(\mathcal{H}_{0}\times\mathcal{H}_{0})\backslash(\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma))
.

For the case (i), one can show that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{G^{2}(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t_{p}}G^{-1}(x)dx\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\int_{1}^{t_{p}}(x+\frac{1}{x})e^{\frac{x^{2}}{2}}dx+\frac{1}{G(1)}\right]\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\sqrt{2\pi}\int_{1}^{t_{p}}xe^{\frac{x^{2}}{2}}dx+\sqrt{2\pi e}\right]\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}m\sqrt{\log\log m}}{\sqrt{\log m}}.

Since
|\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})|\leq Cp^{2}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{0}^{2}}\sum_{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})}\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\leq C\frac{\sqrt{\log\log p}}{\sqrt{\log p}}+Cp^{-c}\rightarrow 0.

Now we present a lemma on the tail bound of bivariate Gaussian distribution, which will be useful for the other two cases.

Lemma 10
.

Suppose that
(z_{1},z_{2})^{\top}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\begin{pmatrix}1&\rho\\
\rho&1\end{pmatrix})
.

•

If
|\rho|\leq C(\log p)^{-2-\gamma}
for some
\gamma>0
, then

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq x\leq\sqrt{C\log p}}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}(|z_{1}|\geq x,|z_{2}|\geq x)}{G^{2}(x)}-1\right|\leq C(\log p)^{-1-\min\{\gamma,\frac{1}{2}\}}.

(66)

•

If
|\rho|\leq\rho_{0}<1
for some
\rho_{0}>0
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|z_{1}|\geq t,|z_{2}|\geq t)\leq C(t+1)^{-2}\exp\{-t^{2}/(1+\rho_{0})\}

(67)

holds uniformly over
0\leq t\leq\sqrt{C\log p}
.

Lemma
10
is a direct implication of Lemma 6.1 and Lemma 6.2 in
[
36
]
.
For the case (ii), we apply (
67
) to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{(x_{k}+1)^{2}}{(x_{k}^{\prime}+1)^{2}}\exp\left\{\frac{\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}x_{k}^{2}+\frac{x_{k}^{2}-x_{k}^{\prime 2}}{1+\rho_{0}}\right\},

where the second line is due to that
G(x_{k})\geq\frac{C}{(x_{k}+1)}e^{-\frac{x_{k}^{2}}{2}}
. Further note that

\displaystyle x_{k}^{2}-x_{k}^{\prime 2}\leq 2(x_{k}-x_{k}^{\prime})x_{k}\leq 2t_{p}^{2}\max\{\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)},\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(2)}\}+2t_{p}\max\{\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)},\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(1)}\}+2t_{p}(\log p)^{-2}\rightarrow 0,

which then implies

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{k=1}^{K}\exp\left\{\frac{\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}x_{k}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log m\log\log m}\int_{0}^{t_{p}}\exp\left\{\frac{\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}x^{2}\right\}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cm^{\frac{2\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}}(\log m)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}}\sqrt{\log\log m}.

Therefore, Assumption
6
suggests

\sum_{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)}\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=o(m^{2}).

While for the case (iii), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k}^{\prime})}-1\right|\left|\frac{G^{2}(x_{k}^{\prime})}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right|+\left|\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(|z_{1}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime},|z_{2}|\geq x_{k}^{\prime}\right)}{G^{2}(x_{k}^{\prime})}-1\right|+\left|\frac{G^{2}(x_{k}^{\prime})}{G^{2}(x_{k})}-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\log p)^{-1-\min\{\gamma,\frac{1}{2}\}}+C\frac{\phi(x_{k}^{\prime})(x_{k}-x_{k}^{\prime})}{G(x_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\log\log p}(\log p)^{-\min\{\gamma,\frac{1}{2}\}}+C\log p\sqrt{\log\log p}(t_{p}\max\{\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(2)},\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(2)}\}+\max\{\varepsilon_{a,b}^{(1)},\varepsilon_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}^{(1)}\}+(\log p)^{-2}),

which converges to zero by Assumption
5
. Therefore,

\sum_{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\notin\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})\cup\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)}\mathrm{II}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=o(m^{2}).

Now we have shown that (
65
) converges to zero for any
\epsilon>0
, and hence (
63
) holds. The proof of Theorem
5
is complete.

I.7
Proof of Supporting Lemmas and Auxiliary Propositions

Before presenting the proofs of the supporting lemmas, here we first introduce the notions of sub-Gaussian and sub-exponential random variables,
\psi_{\alpha}
norm, which was introduced in
[
1
]
as a generalization of the sub-Gaussian and sub-exponential norms when
\alpha=2
or
\alpha=1
.

Definition 1

(
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm
[
1
,
53
]
)
.

The
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm of any random variable
X
and
\alpha>0
is defined as
\|X\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}:=\inf\{C\in(0,\infty):\mathbb{E}[\exp\{(|X|/C)^{\alpha}\}]\leq 2\}

The The following two lemmas from
[
23
]
provide useful properties for product of random variables with bounded
\psi_{\alpha}
norm.

Lemma 11

(
\psi_{\alpha}
norm of product of r.v.s
[
23
]
)
.

Suppose
X_{1},\dots,X_{m}
are
m
random variables (not necessarily independent) with
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm bounded by
\|X_{j}\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq K_{j}
. Then the
\psi_{\alpha/m}
-norm of
\prod_{j=1}^{m}X_{j}
is bounded as
\|\prod_{j=1}^{m}X_{j}\|_{\psi_{\alpha/m}}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}K_{j}
.

Lemma 12

(Concentration of sum of r.v.s with bounded
\psi_{\alpha}
-norm
[
23
,
46
]
)
.

Suppose
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
are independent random variables satisfying
\|X_{i}\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}\leq b
. Then there exists absolute constant
C(\alpha)
only depending on
\alpha
such that for any
a=(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
0<\delta<1/e^{2}
,

\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}X_{i}-\mathbb{E}(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}X_{i})\right|\leq C(\alpha)b\|a\|_{2}(\log\delta^{-1})^{1/2}+C(\alpha)b\|a\|_{\infty}(\log\delta^{-1})^{1/\alpha}

with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Lemma
12
is a direct combination of Lemma 6 in
[
23
]
and Proposition 5.16 in
[
46
]
.

Proof of Lemma
2
.

We first provide an entry-wise error bound for

\left|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}\right|=\left|\frac{1}{n_{j,k}}\sum_{i:j,k\in V_{i}}(x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k})\right|,

over
1\leq j,k\leq p
.

Since
\frac{x_{i,j}}{\sqrt{\Sigma^{*}_{j,j}}}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we have
\|x_{i,j}\|_{\psi_{2}}\leq C_{1}\sqrt{\Sigma^{*}_{j,j}}
for some universal constant
C_{1}

[
46
, see e.g., Lemma 5.14 in]
. By Lemma
11
, for each
(j,k)
,
\|x_{i,j}x_{i,k}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{1}^{2}\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
12
, we have

|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}|\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}}},

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-c\log p\}
.

By the definition of
\widetilde{\Sigma}
in (1), we know that

\begin{split}\sqrt{n_{j,k}}|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}|\leq&\max\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|\\
\leq&\max\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\Sigma^{*}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|,\end{split}

hence we can bound
|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}|
as follows:

\begin{split}|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}|\leq&|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}|+|\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,k}-\widehat{\Sigma}_{j,k}|\\
\leq&\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}_{j,k}}\max\limits_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\sqrt{n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\widehat{\Sigma}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|\\
\leq&C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,k}}}.\end{split}

∎

Proof of Lemma
4
.

By the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{1}
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{1})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j^{\prime}}\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,k^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}})\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}}+\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}}(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,k^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}})+(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{j,j^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}})(\widetilde{\Sigma}_{k,k^{\prime}}-\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}}).

By Lemma
2
, with probability at least
1-Cp^{-c}
,

\displaystyle|(\mathcal{E}_{1})_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{j,j^{\prime}}}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log p}{n_{k,k^{\prime}}}}+\frac{\log p}{\sqrt{n_{j,j^{\prime}}n_{k,k^{\prime}}}}\right)

holds for all
j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}\in[p]
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5
.

First we can decompose the error tensor as follows:

\displaystyle[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2}+\mathcal{D}_{3}+\mathcal{D}_{4},

where
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is the sum of error terms of the
i
th order (including product of
i
error terms). In particular,

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{1}=&(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\\
&+(u^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\\
&+(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\\
&+(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes\epsilon^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}.\end{split}

(68)

We will prove an upper bound for
\|\mathcal{D}_{1}\|_{1}
and show that all higher-order terms can be controlled by
C\|\mathcal{D}_{1}\|_{1}
for some universal constant
C
.

Now we bound each term in (
68
). For the first term,

\displaystyle\|(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}||u^{(2)}_{k}||u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}||u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{n_{j,k}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}||u^{(2)}_{k}||u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}||u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{|u^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(2)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}

where the third line is due to that
n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\leq n_{j,k},n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}
. Following similar arguments to the above, we have

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(1)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{|\epsilon^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}\sum_{j^{\prime}}\frac{|\epsilon^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\frac{\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(4)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

and

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes\epsilon^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}\frac{\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(3)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\frac{|\epsilon^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}.

Combining the bounds above, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{D}_{1}|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(i_{l})}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(i_{l})})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq l,i_{l}}\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}\right].

While for the higher order terms, we can still apply the same argument. For instance,
(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}
is one term in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and it satisfies

\displaystyle\|(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{\epsilon^{(2)}_{k}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(2)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1},

where in the last line we have utilized the condition that

\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in[p]}n_{j,k}}}\right|\leq C_{1}\frac{\|u^{(l)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(l)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}.

While for
(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}
,

\displaystyle\|(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{u^{(2)}_{k}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|\epsilon^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{u^{(2)}_{k}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1},

where the last line is due to that
\|\epsilon^{(l)}\|_{1}\leq C_{1}\|u^{(l)}\|_{1}
. By applying similar arguments to all terms in
\mathcal{D}_{2}
,
\mathcal{D}_{3}
and
\mathcal{D}_{4}
, our proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Assume that all conditions in Lemma
5
hold, and let
j_{l}\in\{1,\dots,4\}
satisfy
j_{l}\equiv l+2(\mathrm{mod}\ 4)
, then we have

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(i_{l})}\|_{1}\|u^{(j_{l})}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(i_{l})})\cup\mathrm{supp}(u^{(j_{l})})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}\prod_{m\neq l,i_{l},j_{l}}\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}\right],

\displaystyle\|[(u^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(1)})\otimes(u^{(2)}+\epsilon^{(2)})\otimes(u^{(3)}+\epsilon^{(3)})\otimes(u^{(4)}+\epsilon^{(4)})

\displaystyle-u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)}]\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\sum_{l=1}^{4}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\left|\epsilon^{(l)}_{j}\right|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq l}\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{j\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}\right],

where
C_{2}
is also a universal constant.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Lemma
5
. In the following, we only show the detailed upper bounds for the first-order error terms (4 terms for each of the two cases, with
\mathcal{N}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{N}^{(2)}
respectively), while the higher-order error terms can be bounded as a constant factor multiplying the first-order error bounds using the same arguments as in the the proof of Lemma
5
.

In particular, one can show that one of the first-order error term satisfies

\displaystyle\|(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{\sqrt{n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}||u^{(2)}_{k}||u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}||u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{n_{j,k}\sqrt{n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}||u^{(2)}_{k}||u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}||u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{|u^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{j^{\prime}}\frac{|u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\frac{\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(2)})\cup\mathrm{supp}(u^{(3)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1},

where we applied
n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\leq n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}
on the third line, and

\displaystyle\|(\epsilon^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\frac{1}{n_{j,k}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}||u^{(2)}_{k}||u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}||u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{|u^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{j^{\prime}}\frac{|u^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\frac{|u^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j}\frac{|\epsilon^{(1)}_{j}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq 1}\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}.

Similarly, one can show that

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(1)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}\|u\|_{3}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(1)})\cup\mathrm{supp}(u^{(3)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k}\frac{|\epsilon^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\|u^{(4)}\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}\sum_{j^{\prime}}\frac{|\epsilon^{(3)}_{j^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\frac{\|u^{(4)}\|_{1}\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(4)})\cup\mathrm{supp}(u^{(2)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}},

and

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes\epsilon^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N})\circ\mathcal{N}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}\|_{1}\frac{\|u^{(3)}\|_{1}\|u^{(2)}\|_{1}}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(3)})\cup\mathrm{supp}(u^{(2)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}}\sum_{k^{\prime}}\frac{|\epsilon^{(4)}_{k^{\prime}}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j^{\prime}}n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}}};

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes\epsilon^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\frac{|\epsilon^{(2)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq 2}\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}},

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes\epsilon^{(3)}\otimes u^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\frac{|\epsilon^{(3)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq 3}\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}},

\displaystyle\|(u^{(1)}\otimes u^{(2)}\otimes u^{(3)}\otimes\epsilon^{(4)})\circ\mathcal{N}^{(2)}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\frac{|\epsilon^{(4)}_{k}|}{\sqrt{\min\limits_{j}n_{j,k}}}\prod_{m\neq 4}\frac{\|u^{(m)}\|_{1}}{\min\limits_{k\in\mathrm{supp}(u^{(m)})}\min\limits_{k}n_{j,k}}.

Hence the first-order error terms for both cases (
\mathcal{N}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{N}^{(2)}
) are bounded accordingly.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6
.

Lemma
6
is a more general version of Lemma
5
and
13
, where the upper bound conditions for
\epsilon^{(l)}
,
l=1,2,3,4
are not assumed. It can be directly proved using exactly the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
5
and
13
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1
.

Recall the definition of
S_{2}(a,b)
in (
29
). Note that if
(j,k)\notin\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}
and
(j,k)\notin\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, one has

\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}=0
and hence
(j,k)\notin S_{2}(a,b)
. This implies that
S_{2}(a,b)\subseteq(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b})\times(\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a})
. If
S_{2}(a,b)\neq(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b})\times(\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a})
, then there exists
(j,k)\in(\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}\times\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{(a)}_{b})
such that
\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}=-\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}\neq 0
, which defines a set of measure zero.

While for
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}
, by definition,
j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}
if and only if

\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,b}\neq 0
. If
b\notin\mathcal{N}_{a}
,
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=0
, and this condition is equivalent to
\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\neq 0
and hence
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}=\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}
.

Otherwise, if
j\notin\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
, one has
\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,a}-\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,b}\neq 0
which implies
j\notin\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}
. Thus
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
. In this case,
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}^{(b)}\neq\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
only happens when there exists
j\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{b}\cup\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{a}
such that
\Theta^{*}_{b,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,a}=\Theta^{*}_{a,j}\Theta^{*}_{a,b}
, which also defines a set of measure zero.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7
.

First note that we can write
\frac{E(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\epsilon_{i}(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}
, where

\epsilon_{i}(a,b)=\sum_{j,k}(x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k})\frac{\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}\frac{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}}{2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}},

and hence
\{\frac{\epsilon_{i}(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are independent mean zero random variables. The following Gaussian approximation result for sum of independent r.v.s is useful for our proof:

Lemma 14

(Theorem 1.1 in
[
58
]
)
.

Let
\tau>O
and
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
be independent mean zero random vectors such that

|\mathbb{E}(\langle\xi_{i},t\rangle^{2}\langle\xi_{i},u\rangle^{m-2})|\leq\frac{1}{2}m!\tau^{m-2}\|u\|^{m-2}\mathbb{E}(\langle\xi_{i},t\rangle^{2})

(69)

holds for
m\geq 3
,
t,u\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and
i=1,\dots,n
. Let
S=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}
. Denote by
Z\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
a multivariate Gaussian random variable with the zero mean and the same covariance as that of
S
. Then for all
\varepsilon>0
,

\sup_{B\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}}\max\{\mathbb{P}(S\in B)-\mathbb{P}(Z\in B^{\varepsilon}),\mathbb{P}(Z\in B)-\mathbb{P}(S\in B^{\varepsilon})\}\leq C_{1}(k)\exp\{-\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{2}(k)\tau}\},

where
B^{\epsilon}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\inf_{y\in B}\|x-y\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon\}
for any set
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{k}
.

In the following, we will let
\xi_{i}(a,b)=\frac{\epsilon_{i}(a,b)}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}
and show that
\xi_{i}(a,b)
satisfies (
69
). Recall the
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{\alpha}}
norm in Definition
1
, and by the equivalence between some properties of sub-exponential random variables
[see
46
, Definition 5.13]
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}(\langle\xi_{i}(a,b),t\rangle^{2}\langle\xi_{i}(a,b),u\rangle^{m-2})|\leq

\displaystyle t^{2}|u|^{m-2}\mathbb{E}|\xi_{i}(a,b)|^{m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle t^{2}|u|^{m-2}m^{m}\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|u|^{m-2}t^{2}\frac{1}{2}m!\tau_{i}^{m-2}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b)),

where
\tau_{i}=12\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\frac{\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))}\vee 1\right)
. The last line is due to that

\frac{2m^{m}}{m!}\leq\frac{2e^{m}}{\sqrt{2\pi m}}\leq\frac{e^{m}}{\sqrt{m}}\leq(\frac{e^{3}}{\sqrt{3}})^{m-2}\leq 12^{m-2},

where the first inequality is due to Stirling’s formula:
m!\geq\sqrt{2\pi m}(\frac{m}{e})^{m}
, and

m^{m}\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{m}\leq\frac{1}{2}m!12^{m-2}\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{m-2}\frac{\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))\leq\frac{1}{2}m!\left(12\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\frac{\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))}\vee 1\right)\right)^{m-2}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b)).

Now it suffices to show that each
\tau_{i}\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}^{3}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}}{\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}
. Recall that we have defined
U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}=\frac{U_{j,k}\delta^{(i)}_{j,k}}{n_{j,k}}
, where
U_{j,k}=\frac{\Theta^{(a)*}_{j,b}\Theta^{*}_{k,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{k,b}\Theta^{*}_{j,a}}{2\Theta^{*}_{a,a}}
in the proof of Theorem
2
. Then we can also write
\xi_{i}(a,b)=\frac{1}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)}\sum_{j,k}(x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k})U^{(\delta,i)}_{j,k}
. It has been shown in the proof of Theorem
2
that
\sigma_{n}(a,b)\geq\sqrt{2}\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}}
. Since
\|x_{i,j}x_{i,k}-\Sigma^{*}_{j,k}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}
, as has been shown in the proof of Lemma
2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}\|U^{(\delta,i)}\|_{1}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)\|U^{(\delta,i)}\|_{F}}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})}\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-2}}{\sum_{j,k}U_{j,k}^{2}n_{j,k}^{-1}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}.

Furthermore, we can upper bound
\frac{\|\xi_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2})}
as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))}=

\displaystyle\frac{\|\epsilon_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\epsilon_{i}(a,b)^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{2}_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2}\|U^{(\delta,i)}\|_{F}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})\|U^{(\delta,i)}\|_{F}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{2}_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma^{*})}.

Therefore, we have shown that
\tau_{i}=12\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}\left(\frac{\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}(\xi_{i}^{2}(a,b))}\vee 1\right)\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|^{3}_{\infty}(d_{a}+d_{b}+1)^{3}}{\lambda_{\min}^{3}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}}}
, and the proof is now complete.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

Similar to the proof of Lemma
7
, we will apply the Gaussian approximation bound in Lemma
14
by validating (
69
) for
(\xi_{i}(a,b),\xi_{i}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

For any two-dimensional random vector
X=(X_{1},X_{2})^{\top}
, and
t,u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\langle X,t\rangle^{2}\langle X,u\rangle^{m-2})\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{2m-4}}\|u\|_{2}^{m-2}\|t\|_{2}^{2},

and for any integer
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{2k}\leq\mathbb{E}(X_{1}^{2}+X_{2}^{2})^{k}\leq 2^{k-1}(\mathbb{E}|X_{1}|^{2k}+\mathbb{E}|X_{2}|^{2k})\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k-1}(2k)^{2k}(\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2k}+\|X_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\sqrt{2}k)^{2k}(\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}\vee\|X_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}})^{2k}

where the second inequality is due to the Jensen’s inequality, the third inequality is due to the property of
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
norm. Hence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\langle X,t\rangle^{2}\langle X,u\rangle^{m-2})\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{4}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|X\|_{2}^{2m-4}}\|u\|_{2}^{m-2}\|t\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32(2\sqrt{2}m)^{m-2}(\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}\vee\|X_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}})^{m}\|u\|_{2}^{m-2}\|t\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{32}{m^{2}}[2\sqrt{2}(\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}\vee\|X_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}})]^{m-2}\frac{\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}\vee\|X_{2}\|_{\psi_{1}}^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}(\mathrm{Cov}(X))}\lambda_{\min}(\mathrm{Cov}(X))m^{m}\|u\|_{2}^{m-2}\|t\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}m!\|u\|_{2}^{m-2}\tau^{m-2}\mathbb{E}(\langle X,t\rangle^{2}),

where
\tau=\frac{120(\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}}\vee\|X_{1}\|_{\psi_{1}})^{3}}{\lambda_{\min}(\mathrm{Cov}(X))}
, and we have applied the fact that
\frac{2m^{m}}{m!}\leq(\frac{e^{3}}{\sqrt{3}})^{m-2}
and
\mathbb{E}(\langle X,t\rangle^{2})\geq\|t\|_{2}^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\mathrm{Cov}(X))
on the last line.

Recall the proof of Lemma
7
and the definition of
\alpha(\Theta^{*},\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})
, we have

\|\xi_{i}(a,b)\|_{\psi_{1}},\|\xi_{i}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d+1)}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}\vee n_{2}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}},

and
(\xi_{i}(a,b),\xi_{i}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime}))^{\top}
satisfies (
69
) with
\tau=\frac{C\|\Sigma^{*}\|_{\infty}(d+1)\alpha(\Theta^{*},\{V_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n})}{\lambda_{\min}(\Sigma^{*})\sqrt{n_{2}^{(a,b)}\vee n_{2}^{(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}}}
. The proof completes with the application of Lemma
14
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4
.

We first derive the closed form solution of
\rho_{n}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
under the simultaneous measurement setting, and then find appropriate super-sets of
\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)
with bounded sizes.

Recall that
\rho_{n}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}{\sigma_{n}(a,b)\sigma_{n}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})}
, where
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}}\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{3}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b^{\prime}}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{:,a^{\prime}}
,
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=\frac{1}{(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}\times_{1}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\times_{2}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\times_{3}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\times_{4}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}
. In the simultaneous measurement setting,
n_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=n_{j,k}=n_{j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=n
for all node
j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}\in[p]
, and hence
\mathcal{T}^{(n)*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n}\mathcal{T}^{*}_{j,k,j^{\prime},k^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{n}(\Sigma^{*}_{j,j^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{k,k^{\prime}}+\Sigma^{*}_{j,k^{\prime}}\Sigma^{*}_{k,j^{\prime}})
. Therefore, one can compute

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b)=&\frac{1}{n(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}\left[\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\right]\\
=&\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{b,b}-(\Theta^{*}_{a,b})^{2}}{n(\Theta^{*}_{a,a})^{2}}.\end{split}

Similarly, one has
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=\frac{\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{b^{\prime},b^{\prime}}-(\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}})^{2}}{n(\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}})^{2}}
. While for
\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})
, note that when
(a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
,
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}}=0
. Thus we have

\begin{split}\sigma_{n}^{2}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=&\frac{1}{n\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}}\left[\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{(a^{\prime})*}_{:,b^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a^{\prime}}+\Theta^{(a)*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a^{\prime}}\Theta^{(a^{\prime})*}_{b^{\prime},:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}}\left[\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,b^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a^{\prime}}+\Theta^{*}_{b,:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{b^{\prime},:}\Sigma^{*}\Theta^{*}_{:,a}\right]\\
=&\frac{\Theta^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}+\Theta^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}}{n\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

where we have applied the fact that
\Theta^{(a)*}_{:,b}=\Theta^{*}_{:,b}
when
\Theta^{*}_{a,b}=0
on the second line. Therefore, for
(a,b),(a^{\prime},b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
,

\begin{split}\rho_{n}(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime})=&\frac{\Theta^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}+\Theta^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{\Theta^{*}_{a,a}\Theta^{*}_{b,b}\Theta^{*}_{a^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{b^{\prime},b^{\prime}}}}\\
=&\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}+\Omega^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})\subset&\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}):|\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}+\Omega^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}|>\rho_{0}\}\\
\subset&\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}):|\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\text{ or }|\Omega^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\}\\
\subset&\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}),(a,b,b^{\prime},a^{\prime}):|\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\text{ and }|\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\}.\end{split}

(70)

Since for
1\leq j\leq p
,
|\{k:|\Omega^{*}_{j,k}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}|\leq\frac{C}{2}
, it is straightforward to see that
|\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}):|\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\text{ and }|\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}|>\frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\}|\leq\frac{C}{2}p^{2}
, and hence
|\mathcal{A}_{1}(\rho_{0})|\leq Cp^{2}
. While for
\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)
, note that

\begin{split}\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)\subset&\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}):|\Omega^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}+\Omega^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}\Omega^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}|>0\}\\
\subset&\{(a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}):\Theta^{*}_{a,a^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{b,b^{\prime}}\neq 0\text{ or }\Theta^{*}_{a,b^{\prime}}\Theta^{*}_{b,a^{\prime}}\neq 0\},\end{split}

(71)

which implies
|\mathcal{A}_{2}(\rho_{0},\gamma)|\leq 2d^{2}p^{2}\ll p^{\frac{4}{1+\rho_{0}}}(\log p)^{\frac{2\rho_{0}}{1+\rho_{0}}-\frac{1}{2}}(\log\log p)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Now the proof is complete.
∎
